禽流感 血凝素 突变 热点驱动跨物种传播和热恢复能力 - Bioengineer.org

Avian Influenza Hemagglutinin Mutation Hotspots Drive Cross-Species Spread and Thermal Resilience - Bioengineer.org
新闻简报Bioengineer.org 发表的一项研究绘制了 禽流感 A(H9N2) 血凝素的适应性景观,确定了 179 个高度可变的替代热点。这些热点控制病毒跨物种潜力、抗体逃避和环境热稳定性。研究表明,家禽是 H9N2 的关键过渡 储存宿主,促进跨物种传播和抗原漂移。研究结果对 疫苗 发展和公共卫生 监测 具有影响。
查看新闻五要素
时间
自 1966 年在威斯康星州首次发现以来,禽流感 A(H9N2) 已通过禽类 宿主 在全球传播了近 60 年。
地点与对象
全球禽类宿主,包括国内和野生鸟类,以及人类临床病例。
事件
一项综合研究绘制了 禽流感 A(H9N2) 血凝素的适应性景观,揭示了控制病毒跨物种潜力、抗体逃避和环境热稳定性的特定氨基酸取代热点。该研究由 Bioengineer.org 报道。
规模、影响与风险
该研究在 H9N2 血凝素中鉴定出 179 个高度可变的取代热点,其中残基 168 显示出最高的熵得分。研究表明,家禽是 H9N2 的关键过渡 储存宿主,促进跨物种传播和抗原漂移。研究结果对 疫苗 发展和公共卫生 监测 具有影响。
应对、状态与不确定性
该研究使用高分辨率实验策略来探究热点变化,克服了传统进化 监测 方法的局限性。该研究结果基于实验室实验,尚未应用于现场。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Avian Influenza Hemagglutinin Mutation Hotspots Drive Cross-Species Spread and Thermal Resilience - Bioengineer.org

News Brief
A study published by Bioengineer.org mapped the adaptive landscape of avian influenza A(H9N2) hemagglutinin, identifying 179 highly variable substitution hotspots. These hotspots govern viral cross-species potential, antibody evasion, and environmental thermostability. The research suggests that domestic poultry serve as a critical transitional reservoir for H9N2, facilitating cross-species spread and antigenic drift. The findings have implications for vaccine development and public health surveillance.
Original source text (English)
HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER Friday, November 13, 2026 No Result View All Result Login HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs Lecturer PhD Studentship Postdoc Research Assistant EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs Lecturer PhD Studentship Postdoc Research Assistant EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER No Result View All Result No Result View All Result Home NEWS Science News Health Avian Influenza Hemagglutinin Mutation Hotspots Drive Cross-Species Spread and Thermal Resilience by November 12, 2026 in Biology, Health Reading Time: 6 mins read 0 Avian Influenza Hemagglutinin Mutation Hotspots Drive Cross-Species Spread and Thermal Resilience Share on FacebookShare on TwitterShare on LinkedinShare on RedditShare on Telegram A comprehensive study mapping the adaptive landscape of avian influenza A(H9N2) hemagglutinin has revealed how specific amino acid substitution hotspots govern viral cross-species potential, antibody evasion, and environmental thermostability. Avian influenza A(H9N2) has circulated globally across avian hosts for nearly six decades following its initial detection in Wisconsin in 1966. Over subsequent decades, the virus has established widespread endemicity in poultry populations across multiple geographic regions, creating persistent agricultural burdens and public health concerns. Beyond domestic avian infections, H9N2 serves as an established reservoir capable of infecting diverse animal hosts and donating internal gene segments to emerging zoonotic influenza strains, including H5N1 and H7N9. In many poultry-dense settings, H9N2 has become the predominant subtype, complicating disease control because conventional inactivated vaccines frequently fail to provide durable protection against divergent field strains. To address these evolutionary challenges, investigators systematically mapped the genetic determinants within the viral surface glycoprotein that enable H9N2 to alter antigenicity, adapt to mammalian cells, and endure thermal stress. The viral hemagglutinin surface glycoprotein mediates host cell entry and represents the primary target of neutralizing antibodies, placing it at the center of antigenic drift and vaccine formulation. Over prolonged circulation, H9N2 hemagglutinin has diversified into major phylogenetic lineages, including Y439, G1, and BJ94, with BJ94 further dividing into more than sixteen antigenically distinct subgroups exhibiting limited cross-reactivity. To resolve the specific mutational patterns driving this diversity, investigators examined 13,984 full-length hemagglutinin coding sequences retrieved from public databases through June 2025. This curated sequence repository encompassed isolates from four primary host groups: 13,014 sequences from domestic avians, 837 from wild birds, 110 from human clinical cases, and 23 from swine. By aligning these coding sequences with multiple sequence alignment algorithms and segregating them by host origin, the researchers established an extensive baseline to evaluate how natural selection operates across different species boundaries. To quantify amino acid variation across the surface glycoprotein, researchers calculated site-specific Shannon entropy values across all 13,984 sequences, establishing an entropy threshold of 0.2 or greater to classify 179 positions as highly variable substitution hotspots. Each identified site harbored at least two distinct amino acid variants, with residues HA66, HA145, and HA168 exhibiting the greatest diversity. Site 168 displayed the highest overall entropy score of 2.63 across fourteen distinct substitutions, followed by site 145 with an entropy value of 1.81. Cross-species correlation analyses revealed that hotspot substitution profiles in avian isolates closely mirrored those in human isolates, yielding a Pearson correlation coefficient of 0.99. Conversely, wild bird isolates displayed the lowest similarity to human isolates, registering a Pearson coefficient of 0.77. At residue 145, serine predominated in wild birds while aspartic acid dominated in poultry and humans. At residue 168, serine predominated in wild birds, alanine in poultry, and human isolates harbored both alanine and asparagine variants, underscoring domestic poultry as an essential transitional reservoir. Discover more Engineering job board Research publication platform Scientific instrument sales Laboratory equipment supplier Learn Engineering Recognizing that conventional evolutionary surveillance methods suffer from intrinsic constraints, the authors developed a high-resolution experimental strategy to interrogate hotspot variation. Standard phylogenetic monitoring remains strictly retrospective, restricted to mutations already established in circulating isolates, while traditional in vitro selections rely upon spontaneous mutagenesis that samples mutational space unevenly. To overcome these limitations, the research team constructed a focused deep mutational scanning library targeting all 179 entropy-defined hotspot sites. Using quick-change site-directed mutagenesis, investigators introduced degenerate random codons into each hotspot position within the hemagglutinin gene of the reverse-genetics plasmid rgBJ16/13, representing the A/chicken/Beijing/16/2013 strain. Plasmid pools generated from at least five hundred bacterial colonies per site were harvested and pooled. The resulting hemagglutinin plasmid library was cotransfected into HEK-293T cells alongside seven complementary genomic plasmids encoding the internal segments of rgBJ16/13 to rescue viable viral progeny, generating a primary passage-zero stock that was subsequently amplified in Madin-Darby canine kidney cells. The resulting deep mutational scanning virus library was evaluated using barcoded subamplicon sequencing with unique molecular identifiers to accurately quantify substitution frequencies. The reverse genetics procedure successfully rescued viral variants across all 179 targeted hotspot positions. While initial rescue efficiency for 91.6 percent of individual hotspot sites proved lower than that of parental wild-type rgBJ16/13, sites HA150, HA198, and HA264 showed significantly enhanced rescue, with HA198 reaching a 2.7-fold increase over wild type. Following amplification, deep sequencing revealed that 94.4 percent of hotspot positions maintained substitution frequencies below one percent, with site HA181 exhibiting the highest frequency at 5.4 percent, alongside elevated frequencies at HA115 and HA179. Comparing sitewise library frequencies to the Shannon entropy of natural strains revealed no significant correlation (Pearson R = 0.092), confirming that the experimental library captured unexplored mutational space beyond circulating strains. In contrast, initial rescue efficiency positively correlated with final amplified frequency (Pearson R = 0.22), confirming the predictive value of rescue dynamics. To identify which hotspot residues mediate escape from humoral immunity, the investigators subjected the viral library to three consecutive rounds of in vitro selection using chicken vaccine antiserum raised against rgBJ16/13 at an eighty percent neutralizing concentration. Deep sequencing performed after three rounds of immune selection revealed that selective pressure focused almost exclusively on substitutions at amino acid position 164. Parallel selection using wild-type virus carrying arginine at position 164 confirmed this focus, enriching for a glutamine substitution across two out of three independent biological replicates. To validate these findings, the authors engineered individual recombinant viruses carrying specific substitutions at position 164 and conducted plaque reduction neutralization tests. Analysis of area under the curve measurements demonstrated that viruses carrying 164Q, 164H, 164V, or 164L escaped neutralization by 164R antiserum, causing an approximately one- to two-fold reduction in neutralizing efficacy. Lineage comparisons further revealed that position 164 possesses distinctive lineage-specific profiles, with arginine dominating Y439, glutamine dominating G1, and BJ94 maintaining both variants, establishing residue 164 as an essential determinant of antigenic drift. The investigation subsequently examined host-dependent viral replication dynamics by serially passaging the mixed hotspot library through five consecutive rounds in two distinct biological environments: mammalian Madin-Darby canine kidney cells and ten-day-old embryonated chicken eggs. Over the five serial passages, viral titers displayed contrasting trajectories, increasing progressively in canine cell culture while showing a relative decrease during propagation in embryonated eggs. Next-generation sequencing performed on fifth-passage viral populations revealed profound, system-specific alterations in substitution profiles. In mammalian cell culture, an asparagine-to-histidine substitution at residue 115 (N115H) underwent rapid positive selection, expanding from baseline library levels to constitute 54.6 percent of the total viral population by the fifth passage. In marked contrast, passaging within embryonated chicken eggs selected for entirely distinct genetic alterations, leading to the enrichment of an S334G substitution to 73.5 percent abundance alongside an L224R substitution at 19.5 percent frequency, demonstrating that host cellular environments impose divergent selective pressures upon the avian influenza mutational repertoire. Statistical correlation analyses of substitution frequencies further illuminated the structural divergence between the two host-passaged viral populations. The fifth-passage population harvested from mammalian cell culture maintained a strong positive correlation with the original input library, registering a Pearson correlation coefficient of 0.84, which indicated that despite the strong enrichment of N115H, broad genetic diversity across other hotspot positions was largely preserved. Conversely, passage through embryonated eggs triggered extensive restructuring of the mutational spectrum, yielding a low Pearson correlation coefficient of 0.14 when compared to the starting library. To confirm whether the dominant mammalian mutation conferred a measurable fitness advantage, researchers assessed multi-cycle replication kinetics in confluent canine kidney cells infected at a multiplicity of infection of 0.005. Growth curve analyses across three independent biological replicates demonstrated that recombinant viruses bearing the N115H substitution attained significantly higher infectious titers than parental wild-type rgBJ16/13 at 12, 24, and 36 hours post-infection, establishing that this hotspot substitution enhances viral replication efficiency in mammalian systems. In addition to host adaptation and immune evasion, the researchers evaluated hemagglutinin environmental stability, an essential physical parameter that influences viral persistence, transmission dynamics, and zoonotic potential. To identify substitutions increasing structural resilience, the 179-pool hotspot library was subjected to thermal selection at 52.5 degrees Celsius for 30 minutes, an exposure that inactivates over 90 percent of wild-type rgBJ16/13. Normalized plaque assays revealed that substitution pools at positions 14, 134, 218, 302, and 436 conferred markedly enhanced heat tolerance. Deep sequencing of eleven heat-selected sites—positions 4, 13, 14, 40, 134, 176, 218, 230, 302, 306, and 436—demonstrated a drastic reduction in sequence entropy, yielding single, heat-resistant amino acids. To validate these thermal phenotypes, authors tested purified viral variants carrying I134L and L230S within the receptor-binding domain and V306L within the stem region. Inactivation assays across a temperature gradient from 50 to 55 degrees Celsius confirmed that all three substitutions consistently increased heat resistance relative to wild-type virus. By linking computational entropy calculations derived from natural sequence databases directly to high-throughput experimental mutagenesis and phenotypic selection, this research establishes an integrated functional map of avian influenza hemagglutinin evolvability. The authors noted that whereas residue 164 exhibits extensive polymorphism among circulating H9N2 viruses, this identical position remains strictly conserved across other avian influenza subtypes such as H5 and H7, illustrating why reliance on passive sequence monitoring alone fails to anticipate emerging evolutionary trajectories. Furthermore, the exclusion of conserved epitopes from the library ensured that experimental screening focused specifically on regions possessing genuine adaptive plasticity rather than structural fragility. These findings demonstrate that H9N2 antigenic diversity, cross-species replication competence, and environmental stability are orchestrated by a discrete set of mutable hotspots that can be systematically identified and functionally characterized prior to their widespread emergence in nature. Consequently, the authors conclude that this high-throughput deep mutational scanning framework provides an essential scientific platform for predictive viral risk assessment, proactive molecular surveillance of field strains, and the rational engineering of stabilized, broadly protective next-generation poultry vaccines. 禽流感造成了灾难性的死亡——接下来会出现一波灭绝浪潮吗? - 卫报

Bird flu has caused catastrophic die-offs – could a wave of extinctions follow? - The Guardian
新闻简报高致病性禽流感 (H5N1) 暴发 导致南象海豹和鹈鹕灾难性死亡,导致南象海豹的保护状况发生变化,并对至少 40 种鸟类产生潜在影响。科学家正在重新评估受影响物种的保护状况,数据收集和发布的滞后影响了灭绝风险评估的准确性。
查看新闻五要素
时间
北方塘鹅的遗体(可能是 禽流感 的受害者)被冲到海滩上——据国际鸟盟称,H5N1 现在对至少 40 种鸟类的保护构成威胁。
地点与对象
南乔治亚岛,南大西洋殖民地,秘鲁。
事件
H5N1 禽流感导致南象海豹和鹈鹕灾难性死亡,南象海豹的保护状况发生变化,并对其他物种产生潜在影响。
规模、影响与风险
南乔治亚岛有超过 53,000 只雌性象海豹死亡,秘鲁至少有 13,000 只鸟类死亡。H5N1病毒已传播到五个主要南象海豹种群中的四个,全球南象海豹种群数量减少了近三分之一。该病毒目前对至少 40 种鸟类构成威胁。
应对、状态与不确定性
科学家们正在重新评估受影响物种的保护状况,重点关注大贼鸥、北方塘鹅、秘鲁鹈鹕和洪堡企鹅。数据收集和发布存在滞后,这可能会影响灭绝风险评估的准确性。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Bird flu has caused catastrophic die-offs – could a wave of extinctions follow? - The Guardian

News Brief
A highly pathogenic avian influenza (H5N1) outbreak has caused catastrophic die-offs of southern elephant seals and pelicans, leading to conservation status changes for southern elephant seals and potential impacts on at least 40 bird species. Scientists are reassessing the conservation status of affected species, with a lag in data collection and publication affecting the accuracy of extinction risk assessments.
Original source text (English)
The remains of a northern gannet, probably a victim of avian influenza, washed up on a beach – H5N1 is now a conservation threat to at least 40 bird species, according to BirdLife International. Photograph: Matthijs Wetterauw/Alamy The remains of a northern gannet, probably a victim of avian influenza, washed up on a beach – H5N1 is now a conservation threat to at least 40 bird species, according to BirdLife International. Photograph: Matthijs Wetterauw/Alamy Bird flu has caused catastrophic die-offs – could a wave of extinctions follow? As the IUCN global conservation body prepares to update its red list of endangered species, scientists explain why calculating the true toll of a pandemic is so hard I t began with the brown skuas. The migratory seabirds had arrived on the sub-Antarctic island of South Georgia in 2023, harbouring a deadly passenger. Within months, the southern elephant seals that had come ashore to breed became sick. Mothers and newborn pups were weak and trembling, with many struggling to breathe. What followed was a horrific mass casualty event, caused by the highly infectious avian influenza (H5N1). By 2025, more than 53,000 female elephant seals had died on South Georgia. That is especially alarming, scientists say, because the island is home to more than half of the global breeding population . A highly potent strain of the bird flu virus, which first began circulating in the northern hemisphere in 2021, has now spread to four of the five main southern elephant seal populations. In some South Atlantic colonies, more than 90% of newborn pups have died , alongside about half of all adult females. A southern elephant seal with a pup at Gold harbour, South Georgia. By 2025, more than 53,000 female elephant seals had died on the island. Photograph: Ashley Cooper/Alamy “This is a horror show for wildlife,” says Ruth Cromie, coordinator of a UN taskforce on avian influenza and wildlife. “The scale of mortality is absolutely unprecedented.” In April, the mass deaths prompted the IUCN red list, the global authority responsible for assessing the extinction risks of wildlife, to downgrade the southern elephant seal’s conservation status by two categories, from “least concern” to “vulnerable”. It was the first time a species has had its extinction risk raised explicitly because of H5N1. Dead elephant seals on South Georgia in January 2026. Photograph: Jane Younger The IUCN listing notes that scientists suspect the global southern elephant seal population has declined by nearly a third, and models suggest many populations will take decades to recover. So far, scientists have found few antibodies to the disease among surviving animals. The southern elephant seal is just one species that has suffered catastrophic die-offs since H5N1 began burning its way through the world’s wildlife, killing hundreds of thousands of animals , if not millions. Avian influenza is now a threat to at least 40 bird species, according to BirdLife International , a global conservation partnership. And yet, no other animal – seal or bird – has seen its conservation status changed. But scientists warn that does not mean there are not more species being pushed to the brink by this deadly pathogen. A ssessing a species’ conservation threats is a painstaking scientific and bureaucratic process. To date, the red list has analysed nearly 176,000 species’ extinction risks, and aims to reach 260,000 species by 2030, as well as 142,000 reassessments. Most species are only reassessed by a panel of expert scientists once every decade or so – no matter the emerging threats they may face. The red list itself is updated two to three times a year. Birds are the exception. BirdLife International serves as the authority for assessing the world’s 11,000-plus bird species, reviewing hundreds of birds every year. For that reason, our understanding of the extinction risks of birds is better than for any other animal group. In an epidemic where birds are the foremost victim, that is obviously convenient. But there can still be a lag when deaths are coming faster than scientists can convene and publish under peer review. “We still need to get these things out there,” says Rob Martin, senior red list officer at BirdLife International. Northern gannets rest next to dead gannets on Rouzic Island, off the Brittany coast, in 2022. Photograph: F Tanneau/AFP/Getty Species are generally “uplisted” when they have experienced a sustained population decline over three generations, a small population is in decline or living within a restricted range, or calculations show that the species could become extinct in the near future, given existing population dynamics. Scientific uncertainty often underlies such reviews. Assessors need to know the animal’s baseline population before they can determine if it has crossed a threshold towards extinction. For many species, such figures are in short supply. And without knowing a species’ starting point, it is difficult to calculate the true toll of an outbreak. Scientists with BirdLife International say they are now focused on reassessing four species that have suffered H5N1 outbreaks large enough to affect their conservation outlook: the great skua, northern gannet, Peruvian pelican and Humboldt penguin. Great skuas courting on the Shetland Islands, off Scotland. Photograph: BSIP/Universal/Getty In Scotland, home to more than half of the world’s great skuas , the population of monitored colonies fell by 76% between 2021 and 2023. Meanwhile, the northern gannet, the largest seabird native to the North Atlantic, declined dramatically in two breeding populations in the UK as bird flu spread: by 43% at Bass Rock in Scotland between 2021 and 2024 and 47% at Grassholm in Wales between 2015 and 2024. These are two of the largest northern gannet colonies, and extrapolating from such figures would imply a steep population fall, say BirdLife International assessors, potentially qualifying the species for uplisting from “least concern”. But when scientists took a broader look , they found that the entire north-eastern Atlantic population had declined no more than 17% between 2013 and 2024. “Rushing to judgment a few years ago, with only partial data available and no sense of whether the whole population was declining, would have been premature and could have led to a misclassification,” BirdLife International scientists say. Martin cautions that it is important to prevent “kneejerk” uplisting after a mass mortality event, as listings such as “critically endangered” can trigger dozens of conservation funding proposals and divert government spending. “We need it to be a tool that the community can reliably use and say ‘we trust this,’” he says. Municipal workers collect dead pelicans in Lima, Peru, in November 2022 as another struggles to move. At least 13,000 birds had died by then along Peru’s Pacific coast. Photograph: G Pardo/AP About a fifth of the pelican population living in Peru’s marine protected areas are thought to have succumbed to bird flu in 2022 and 2023. But the Peruvian pelican is considered an abundant bird. “It’s difficult to get accurate numbers for the population pre- and post- [the H5N1 outbreak] because they’re in quite isolated areas without sufficient funding for monitoring,” says Martin. Counting the dead often poses an even greater hurdle, especially for migratory species that travel vast distances over oceans. “You usually don’t find most of the dead birds. If they die at sea, they’re lost,” says Wouter Langhout, a policy consultant at BirdLife International. The flightless Humboldt penguin, which spends most of its life on land, has made such tallies easier. In 2023, scientists counted nearly 3,000 dead penguins along Chile’s coasts, prompting another review of the already vulnerable species, which was last assessed in 2020. E ven when population and mortality numbers are easy to come by, spectacular mass die-offs – as gruesome as they may be – do not guarantee a species is inching towards extinction. For long-lived seabirds, large waterbirds and elephant seals that are slow to reproduce and late to sexually mature, a large loss of breeding adults can translate into severe population declines in just three generations – a key red-list criterion for uplisting. But fast-reproducing animals or those with abundant populations may be spared such long-term consequences, especially if more adult males die than fertile females. Antarctic fur seals in heavy snow on South Georgia as king penguins pass by. Photograph: Ben Cranke/naturepl.com In the case of the Antarctic fur seal, for example, far more males have succumbed to the disease than breeding females, says Jaume Forcada, a marine mammal expert and population biologist at the British Antarctic Survey who has studied Antarctic fur seals for 25 years. That is because male and female fur seals behave very differently. Whereas the males come ashore for about two months, often battling with one another on beaches, “when females arrive, they pup, and then they go out very quickly. They’re less exposed to other animals apart from the moment when they are rearing their pup,” Forcada says. In turn, “the males were very severely affected, whereas the females were absolutely fine. It’s like it was a completely different species. It hadn’t affected them.” If scientists uncover a population decline that would warrant uplisting, attributing it to avian influenza may not be cut and dried, Martin cautions. For example, BirdLife recently reassessed the African penguin, which was heavily affected by avian influenza. Although it was uplisted to critically endangered in 2024, assessors determined that food loss linked to commercial fisheries and the climate crisis – not bird flu – ultimately drove its decline. The carcass of an endangered Humboldt penguin on a beach in Vina del Mar, Chile, in 2023. Photograph: R Garrido/Reuters The 3,000 dead Humboldt penguins in Chile have also triggered debate among scientists. Penguins often drown in gill nets and 2023 was also an El Niño year, which may have had a hand in the birds’ deaths. “That year could be described as a ‘perfect storm’, which produced highly unfavourable conditions for Humboldt penguins,” says Alejandro Simeone, a scientist in the IUCN’s penguin specialist group. “While it is quite possible that some Humboldt penguins died from [avian influenza], current evidence makes it difficult to assess the true extent of the virus’s role in this decline,” Simeone says. I n the end, it may not be the most visible victims that are in the greatest peril. Birds with tiny isolated populations – often those inhabiting small islands – can quickly move towards extinction even if only a handful of individuals die. “For species that are already under threat and declining for other reasons, and then you put in something like avian influenza, that might be the thing that then pushes them over the edge ,” Cromie says. To date, scientists say they are not yet aware of any such cases from bird flu, but are anxiously monitoring Australia , where H5N1 was first detected on the mainland in June. The endangered regent honeyeater in an aviary at Sydney’s Taronga zoo this year, where vulnerable species of birds are being vaccinated against bird flu. Photograph: B de Marchi/AAP “A lot of Australian species still do have quite wide ranges, but there’s a very specific habitat that they rely on,” Martin says. The country is also home to a number of critically endangered birds that can be found nowhere else in the world, such as the orange-bellied parrot and the regent honeyeater. Waiting for uplisting, Cromie adds, may miss the bigger picture. “We have to be focusing on taking these other pressures off,” she says. “If we become very fixated on only waiting to do something because of a change of status, there’s such a lag there. We’ve got to be doing other things at the same time.” Already, fur seals and dolphins in Australian waters have begun to die from bird flu. And things are about to get worse for the now-vulnerable southern elephant seal. In August, the government’s Australian Antarctic Division said it would evacuate its research station on Macquarie Island in anticipation of mass seal die-offs if H5N1 reaches its shores. Macquarie is the last stronghold of southern elephant seals that, until recently, had remained untouched by the virus. Though the risk to human health is low, the agency said it hoped to prevent psychological distress for staff who could witness the deaths of thousands of newborn seal pups. About 5,000 of the world’s largest little penguin colony, on Australia’s Phillip Island, were vaccinated against H5N1. Photograph: Getty The next update to the list is due on 26 November, and proposals for new uplistings will next be discussed in January and February 2027. “Our biggest fear is always that we completely miss something that suddenly is critically endangered. That’s what we’re guarding against,” Martin says. Find more age of extinction coverage here , and follow the biodiversity reporters Rebecca Ratcliffe , Phoebe Weston and Patrick Greenfield in the Guardian app for more nature coverage. Time
The remains of a northern gannet, probably a victim of avian influenza, washed up on a beach – H5N1 is now a conservation threat to at least 40 bird species, according to BirdLife International.
Location and population
South Georgia, South Atlantic colonies, Peru.
Event
Catastrophic die-offs of southern elephant seals and pelicans due to H5N1 bird flu, with conservation status changes for southern elephant seals and potential impacts on other species.
Scale, impact and risk
More than 53,000 female elephant seals died on South Georgia, and at least 13,000 birds died in Peru. The H5N1 virus has spread to four of the five main southern elephant seal populations, with a decline of nearly a third in the global southern elephant seal population. The virus is now a threat to at least 40 bird species.
Response, status and uncertainty
Scientists are reassessing the conservation status of affected species, with a focus on great skuas, northern gannets, Peruvian pelicans, and Humboldt penguins. There is a lag in data collection and publication, which can affect the accuracy of extinction risk assessments.

罕见的 H9N2 流感 突变 减少病毒复制但加剧肺损伤

Rare H9N2 Influenza Mutation Reduces Viral Replication but Worsens Lung Damage
新闻简报2026 年 11 月 7 日发表的一项研究发现了一种罕见的 H9N2 流感 突变(HAΔ227),它会减少病毒复制,但会增加受感染鸡的肺部损伤。研究人员发现,位于血凝素 220 环内的 突变 对病毒造成了可测量的适应性成本,导致体内病毒脱落减少并减少了呼吸道复制。尽管存在这些限制,突变 仍与更严重的肺部病变和加剧的炎症反应有关,包括干扰素 γ、白细胞介素 10、白细胞介素 1β 和单核细胞趋化蛋白 1 水平升高。研究结果表明,仅监测病毒载量可能无法捕获新出现的 禽流感 变种的全部致病潜力。这项研究是在实验室环境中利用反向遗传学和动物试验进行的,强调了 禽流感 病毒复制效率与疾病严重程度之间的复杂关系。
查看新闻五要素
时间
该研究发表于2026年11月7日,研究结果基于HAΔ227 突变进行的实验,该病毒被鉴定为H9N2流感病毒中罕见的突变。
地点与对象
该研究在实验室环境中使用鸡作为实验动物进行,重点关注 HAΔ227 突变 对 禽流感 的影响。
事件
一种罕见的 H9N2 流感 突变(HAΔ227)因其对病毒复制和疾病严重程度的影响而被表征。研究发现,突变 可以减少病毒复制,但会增加受感染鸡的肺损伤。
规模、影响与风险
据观察,HAΔ227 突变 可以减少体内病毒复制和脱落,但它会导致受感染鸟类更严重的肺部病变和炎症反应升高。这些发现强调了仅监测病毒载量可能会漏掉新出现的 禽流感 变体的致病潜力。
应对、状态与不确定性
该研究利用反向遗传学和实验动物试验来评估 突变 的效果。结果表明存在可测量的健身成本并改变了 免疫应答,但没有报告公共卫生应对或遏制措施。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Rare H9N2 Influenza Mutation Reduces Viral Replication but Worsens Lung Damage

News Brief
A study published on November 7, 2026, identified a rare H9N2 influenza mutation, HAΔ227, which reduces viral replication but increases lung damage in infected chickens. Researchers found that this mutation, located within the hemagglutinin 220-loop, imposes a measurable fitness cost on the virus, resulting in lower in vivo shedding and reduced respiratory replication. Despite these limitations, the mutation was linked to more severe pulmonary lesions and heightened inflammatory responses, including elevated levels of interferon-gamma, interleukin-10, interleukin-1beta, and monocyte chemoattractant protein-1. The findings suggest that monitoring viral loads alone may not capture the full pathogenic potential of emerging avian influenza variants. The study, conducted in a laboratory setting using reverse genetics and animal trials, highlights the complex relationship between viral replication efficiency and disease severity in avian influenza.
Original source text (English)
HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER Friday, November 13, 2026 No Result View All Result Login HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs Lecturer PhD Studentship Postdoc Research Assistant EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs Lecturer PhD Studentship Postdoc Research Assistant EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER No Result View All Result No Result View All Result Home NEWS Science News Health Rare H9N2 Influenza Mutation Reduces Viral Replication but Worsens Lung Damage by November 7, 2026 in Biology, Health Reading Time: 2 mins read 0 Rare H9N2 Influenza Mutation Reduces Viral Replication but Worsens Lung Damage Share on FacebookShare on TwitterShare on LinkedinShare on RedditShare on Telegram Researchers characterized the phenotypic consequences of an amino acid loss at residue 227 within the hemagglutinin receptor-binding domain using isogenic reverse genetics viruses. While deletions within this specific domain represent a notable and rare evolutionary trajectory among genetically diverse H9N2 field strains, the finding demonstrates that viral burden alone does not reliably predict the severity of avian influenza disease. The targeted deletion, designated HAΔ227, is a naturally occurring mutation situated directly inside the hemagglutinin 220-loop, a structural segment that overlaps an essential antigenic epitope. In laboratory evaluations, introducing this deletion imposed a measurable fitness cost on the pathogen. Reverse genetics experiments revealed that the mutant virus exhibited altered functional coordination between viral surface glycoproteins, featuring enhanced hemagglutinin receptor-binding avidity alongside reduced apparent virion-associated neuraminidase activity. This compromised replicative capacity persisted during experimental animal trials, where chickens infected with the mutant strain exhibited reduced respiratory shedding in vivo compared to control strains. Paradoxically, despite lower viral replication and reduced shedding, birds infected with the HAΔ227 virus experienced more extensive pathological damage. Post-infection assessments documented more severe pulmonary lesions in the affected lung tissues. These pathological changes were accompanied by a pronounced dissociation between viral RNA load and clinical disease manifestations, confirming that lower pathogen burdens can coincide with heightened structural tissue injury during avian influenza infections. The disproportionate lung injury observed in infected chickens was linked to a sharply altered host immune response elicited by the mutant pathogen. Tissue and molecular analyses revealed significantly elevated levels of critical inflammatory signaling proteins following infection, specifically interferon-gamma, interleukin-10, interleukin-1beta, and monocyte chemoattractant protein-1. Biochemical characterization further demonstrated that the single-residue deletion at position 227 raised the pH threshold required for hemagglutinin-mediated membrane fusion and substantially altered antigenic relationships. The authors concluded that residue 227 represents a functionally vital site where a single deletion governs an evolutionary trade-off between replication efficiency and virulence. Although HAΔ227 viruses replicate less effectively in vitro and shed less abundantly in vivo, their capacity to induce intensified inflammatory responses and severe tissue damage demonstrates that monitoring viral loads alone may obscure the pathogenic potential of emerging avian influenza variants. Time
The study was published on November 7, 2026, and the research findings were based on experiments conducted with the HAΔ227 mutation, which was identified as a rare mutation in H9N2 influenza viruses.
Location and population
The research was conducted in a laboratory setting using chickens as experimental animals, focusing on the effects of the HAΔ227 mutation on avian influenza.
Event
A rare H9N2 influenza mutation, HAΔ227, was characterized for its effects on viral replication and disease severity. The mutation was found to reduce viral replication but increase lung damage in infected chickens.
Scale, impact and risk
The HAΔ227 mutation was observed to reduce viral replication and shedding in vivo, but it caused more severe pulmonary lesions and elevated inflammatory responses in infected birds. These findings highlight the potential for monitoring viral loads alone to miss the pathogenic potential of emerging avian influenza variants.
Response, status and uncertainty
The study used reverse genetics and experimental animal trials to evaluate the mutation's effects. The results indicate a measurable fitness cost and altered immune responses, but no public health response or containment measures were reported.

B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

禽流感首次袭击俄勒冈州奶牛,华盛顿受感染的奶牛数量已增至数十头

Bird flu hits Oregon dairy cattle for the first time as Washington’s infected herds climb into the dozens
新闻简报俄勒冈州奶牛首次确诊禽流感(禽流感),使俄勒冈州成为第 22 个出现感染牛群的州。在此之前,华盛顿奶牛场出现了首例确诊病例,这些病例与三例人类感染有关,其中一人死亡。华盛顿的 暴发 于 2026 年 10 月 8 日开始,俄勒冈州的牛群通过正在进行的 暴发 进行了识别,在检测前检测结果呈阴性。疾病预防控制中心认为公众面临的风险较低,但接触受感染动物或生奶的人面临较高风险。美国农业部确认俄勒冈州存在 HPAI H5 进化枝 2.3.4.4b,华盛顿州存在 H5N1 基因型 B3.13。该机构正在进行调查和诊断测试,强调生物安全措施和巴氏灭菌对于牛奶供应来说是安全的。此次检测不会改变美国农业部的 HPAI 根除策略。
查看新闻五要素
时间
俄勒冈州的 暴发 于 2026 年 11 月 12 日得到确认,首次在奶牛中检测到。截至 2026 年 11 月 12 日,华盛顿州受感染的牛群与 3 例人类感染有关,其中 1 例死亡。华盛顿州的 暴发 于 2026 年 10 月 8 日开始,并于该日确认了第一例受感染的牛群。俄勒冈州牛群在检测之前一直呈阴性,而检测发生在华盛顿 暴发 几周后。
地点与对象
该事件涉及俄勒冈州和华盛顿州,特别是俄勒冈州的奶牛和华盛顿州的受感染牛群。暴发 已影响多个州的奶牛,华盛顿州报告有 33 头奶牛受到感染。华盛顿州已有三人在接触受感染牛的奶牛场后被感染,其中亚基马县有一人死亡。
事件
俄勒冈州的一个奶牛场首次证实存在禽流感(禽流感),使俄勒冈州成为第 22 个有受感染牛的州。在此之前,华盛顿奶牛场出现首例确诊病例,该病例与 3 例人类感染有关,其中 1 例死亡。华盛顿的 暴发 于 2026 年 10 月 8 日得到确认,俄勒冈州的牛群是通过正在进行的 监测 进行识别的。
规模、影响与风险
三起人类感染事件与乳品店接触有关,其中一人死亡。疾病预防控制中心认为公众面临的风险较低,但因工作或娱乐而接触受感染鸟类或哺乳动物的人面临较高风险。美国农业部确认俄勒冈州牛群中存在 HPAI H5 进化枝 2.3.4.4b,华盛顿州首批牛群中存在 H5N1 基因型 B3.13。
应对、状态与不确定性
美国农业部和动植物卫生检验局正在开展农场调查、诊断测试和流行病学数据收集。疾病预防控制中心建议加强奶牛场的生物安全,并为处理受感染动物或原料奶的工人配备高暴露的个人防护装备。该机构还表示,巴氏灭菌可以有效灭活高致病性禽流感病毒,确保商业牛奶供应的安全。此次检测不会改变美国农业部的 HPAI 根除策略。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Bird flu hits Oregon dairy cattle for the first time as Washington’s infected herds climb into the dozens

News Brief
Bird flu, or avian influenza, has been confirmed in Oregon dairy cattle for the first time, making Oregon the 22nd state with infected herds. This follows the first confirmed cases in Washington’s dairy herds, which were linked to three human infections, including one death. The outbreak in Washington began on October 8, 2026, and the Oregon herd was identified through ongoing surveillance, having tested negative before the detection. The CDC considers the risk to the general public low, but those with exposure to infected animals or raw milk are at higher risk. The USDA confirmed the presence of HPAI H5 clade 2.3.4.4b in Oregon and H5N1 genotype B3.13 in Washington. The agency is conducting investigations and diagnostic testing, emphasizing biosecurity measures and pasteurization as safe for the milk supply. The detection does not alter the USDA’s HPAI eradication strategy.

Advertisement Avian influenza has been confirmed in an Oregon dairy herd for the first time, federal officials said Tuesday, making Oregon the 22nd state with infected cattle weeks after an outbreak in neighboring Washington was linked to 3 human infections, including 1 death.1,2 The herd was identified through Oregon’s ongoing surveillance for the virus in dairy cattle and had consistently tested negative before the detection, the US Department of Agriculture’s Animal and Plant Health Inspection Service (APHIS) said in a news release. The agency did not say where in Oregon the herd is located or whether it is connected to other infected herds.1 As recently as November 2, Oregon agriculture officials said they had no confirmed or suspected cases in dairy cattle, and the state was listed as unaffected under the federal milk testing program.3 Washington confirmed its first infected dairy herds on October 8.4 The Washington State Department of Agriculture has since confirmed the virus in a Benton County herd that was found through routine surveillance before cows became ill, and Yakima County officials have reported 33 infected herds, according to the Associated Press (AP).2,5 Three people in Washington have been infected after exposure at dairies with infected cattle. One, a Yakima County resident with underlying health conditions, died November 1 after being hospitalized, the first US avian influenza death in a year. A CDC spokesperson told the AP that the agency found no changes in that patient’s virus that would make it spread more easily or cause more severe illness.2 Third new state with a HPAI detection since October Oregon is the third state since early October to report its first infected dairy herd, after Washington and Indiana.4,6 Indiana’s case, confirmed November 2, was found through routine milk testing.6 The dairy cattle outbreak began in March 2024, and APHIS said it has seen cases in previously unaffected states this year.1 Advertisement Federal laboratory testing identified HPAI H5 clade 2.3.4.4b in the Oregon herd, and APHIS said it will announce genetic sequencing results when they are available.1 Sequencing of the first Washington herds, which were tested after cattle showed clinical signs, found H5N1 genotype B3.13, the strain behind most US dairy herd cases. APHIS said those detections were consistent with transmission from Idaho, likely through cattle movement.4,7 APHIS said it is working with the Oregon Department of Agriculture on additional on-farm investigations, diagnostic testing, and epidemiological data collection.1 APHIS said the detection does not change USDA’s HPAI eradication strategy. The agency recommends enhanced biosecurity on all dairy farms and urged producers to report livestock with clinical signs, or unusual sick or dead wildlife, to their state veterinarian.1 “The U.S. Food and Drug Administration is confident that pasteurization is effective at inactivating HPAI virus, and that the commercial, pasteurized milk supply is safe,” APHIS said in an announcement.1 The CDC considers the risk to the general public low, but people with job-related or recreational exposure to infected birds or mammals are at higher risk.1 CDC worker-safety guidance lists veterinarians and veterinary staff among those who may be exposed and recommends high-exposure personal protective equipment for tasks such as contact with potentially infected animals or their raw milk.8 References USDA confirms highly pathogenic avian influenza in a dairy herd in Oregon. View five news elements
Time
The outbreak in Oregon was confirmed on November 12, 2026, with the first detection in dairy cattle. Washington’s infected herds were linked to 3 human infections, including 1 death, as of November 12, 2026. The outbreak in Washington began on October 8, 2026, and the first infected herd was confirmed on that date. The Oregon herd had consistently tested negative before the detection, which occurred weeks after the Washington outbreak.
Location and population
The event involves Oregon and Washington states, specifically dairy cattle in Oregon and infected herds in Washington. The outbreak has affected dairy cattle in multiple states, with Washington reporting 33 infected herds. Three people in Washington have been infected after exposure at dairies with infected cattle, including one death in Yakima County.
Event
Bird flu (avian influenza) has been confirmed in an Oregon dairy herd for the first time, making Oregon the 22nd state with infected cattle. This follows the first confirmed cases in Washington’s dairy herds, which were linked to 3 human infections, including 1 death. The outbreak in Washington was confirmed on October 8, 2026, and the Oregon herd was identified through ongoing surveillance.
Scale, impact and risk
Three human infections were linked to exposure at dairies, including one death. The CDC considers the risk to the general public low, but people with job-related or recreational exposure to infected birds or mammals are at higher risk. The USDA confirmed the presence of HPAI H5 clade 2.3.4.4b in the Oregon herd and H5N1 genotype B3.13 in Washington’s first herds.
Response, status and uncertainty
The USDA and APHIS are conducting on-farm investigations, diagnostic testing, and epidemiological data collection. The CDC recommends enhanced biosecurity on dairy farms and high-exposure personal protective equipment for workers handling infected animals or raw milk. The agency also stated that pasteurization is effective at inactivating the HPAI virus, ensuring the commercial milk supply is safe. The detection does not change the USDA’s HPAI eradication strategy.

英国确诊两例新的 H5N1 禽流感病例 - 家禽新闻

Two new H5N1 bird flu cases confirmed in England - Poultry News
新闻简报据《家禽新闻》2026 年 11 月 9 日发表的报道,英格兰确认了两例新的 H5N1 禽流感病例。暴发 促使在全国范围内引入强制住房令,以降低风险。虽然报告强调了确诊病例,但没有具体说明受影响的动物或人类病例的数量。英格兰的家禽业受到越来越严格的审查,官员们将 野生鸟类 的禽流感风险提高到高水平。报告还指出,这种情况是 禽流感 担忧的更广泛背景的一部分,包括最近林肯郡商业鸭群和苏格兰边境产蛋鸭群中的 暴发。回应包括正在进行的调查和确保住房标准的措施,但没有提供有关遏制或 治疗 的进一步细节。

B routine priorityPublic-health news

Two new H5N1 bird flu cases confirmed in England - Poultry News

News Brief
Two new cases of H5N1 bird flu have been confirmed in England, according to a report by Poultry News published on November 9, 2026. The outbreak has prompted the introduction of a mandatory housing order across the country to mitigate the risk. While the report highlights the confirmed cases, it does not specify the number of affected animals or human cases. The poultry industry in England is under increased scrutiny, with officials raising the bird flu risk to high for wild birds. The report also notes that the situation is part of a broader context of avian influenza concerns, including recent outbreaks in commercial duck flocks in Lincolnshire and laying flocks in the Scottish Borders. The response includes ongoing investigations and measures to ensure housing standards, but no further details on containment or treatment are provided.

B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

俄勒冈州记录了首例牛 H5N1 病例;农民工会称华盛顿人禽流感病例报告不足

Oregon logs its first H5N1 case in cows; farmer’s union says human avian flu cases underreported in Washington
新闻简报2026 年 11 月,俄勒冈州报告了首例奶牛场感染 H5N1 禽流感病例,而华盛顿州亚基马谷的人类 H5N1 病例有所增加,包括 1 例死亡和 2 例感染。美国农业部证实了俄勒冈州的检测结果,并表示巴氏灭菌可确保商业牛奶供应的安全,不会对消费者健康构成风险。然而,农场工人联盟 (UFW) 强调,由于担心 ICE 报复,亚基马谷的人类病例报告不足,工人出现了从轻微上呼吸道感染到严重流感样症状的症状。自 2024 年以来,CDC 报告了 74 例人类 H5N1 病例,其中 3 例死亡。美国农业部敦促生产者报告任何患病的牲畜,但没有宣布其策略发生变化。暴发 在过去 30 天内影响了 51 个牛群,其中爱达荷州的两个牛群和俄勒冈州的牛群在过去一周被确定。UFW 还指出,只为工人提供基本的防护装备,这引发了人们对安全和报告做法的担忧。
查看新闻五要素
时间
本周早些时候,2026 年 11 月 12 日。
地点与对象
美国俄勒冈州;亚基马谷,华盛顿,美国;该地区的乳制品工人和牛。
事件
据农场工人联盟 (UFW) 称,俄勒冈州报告了首例奶牛 H5N1 病例,华盛顿州亚基马谷报告人类 H5N1 病例增加,其中 1 人死亡,2 人感染。
规模、影响与风险
自 2024 年以来,已在 22 个州检测到 H5N1。在亚基马谷,过去 30 天内有 51 个牛群受到影响,过去一周在爱达荷州发现了两个牛群。美国农业部确认亚基马县有 1 例 H5N1 病毒死亡和另外 2 例感染,并注意到奶牛的职业暴露。由于巴氏灭菌,对人类健康的风险被认为很低,但 UFW 声称由于担心 ICE 报复而少报。
应对、状态与不确定性
美国农业部强调,此次检测不会改变其策略,并敦促生产者报告患病牲畜。UFW 表示,只向工人提供基本的防护装备,并且由于 ICE 相关的担忧,人们担心报告不足。自 2024 年以来,CDC 报告了 74 例人类 H5N1 病例,其中 3 例死亡。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Oregon logs its first H5N1 case in cows; farmer’s union says human avian flu cases underreported in Washington

News Brief
In November 2026, Oregon reported its first case of H5N1 avian flu in a dairy herd, while Yakima Valley in Washington saw increased human H5N1 cases, including one death and two infections. The USDA confirmed the detection in Oregon and stated that pasteurization ensures the safety of the commercial milk supply, posing no risk to consumer health. However, the Union Farm Workers (UFW) highlighted that human cases in Yakima Valley are underreported due to fears of ICE retaliation, with workers experiencing symptoms ranging from mild upper respiratory infections to severe flu-like symptoms. The CDC reported 74 human H5N1 cases since 2024, including three deaths. The USDA urged producers to report any sick livestock, but no changes to its strategy were announced. The outbreak has affected 51 cattle herds in the last 30 days, with two herds in Idaho and the Oregon herd identified in the past week. The UFW also noted that workers are only provided with basic protective gear, raising concerns about safety and reporting practices.
Original source text (English)

Topics & Projects Antimicrobial Stewardship Chronic Wasting Disease Osterholm Update Podcasts Newsletters About CIDRAP CIDRAP in the News Our Staff Our Director Osterholm in the Press Shop Merchandise Contact Us About Search CLOSE Search Support Menu Oregon logs its first H5N1 case in cows; farmer’s union says human avian flu cases underreported in Washington Stephanie Soucheray, MA Avian Influenza (Bird Flu) Diy13/iStock Share Copied to clipboard Earlier this week the US Department of Agriculture’s Animal and Plant Health Inspection Service (USDA’s APHIS) announced for the first time the detection of H5N1 avian flu in a dairy herd in Oregon. Since 2024, avian flu has been detected in 22 states. Most recently, Washington’s Yakima Valley has reported increased activity. “This detection does not pose a risk to consumer health or affect the safety of the commercial milk supply,” the USDA said. “The U.S. Food and Drug Administration is confident that pasteurization is effective at inactivating HPAI [highly pathogenic avian influenza] virus, and that the commercial, pasteurized milk supply is safe.” According to the USDA, the affected herd was tested as part of Oregon's ongoing surveillance for HPAI in dairy cattle. “Prior to this detection, the herd has consistently tested negative through routine surveillance conducted under the National Milk Testing Strategy program,” the agency said. The USDA said the detection does not change any strategy from the agency and instead emphasized that producers should report any livestock with clinical signs or any unusual sick or dead wildlife to their state veterinarian. Union says H5N1 underreported in Yakima Valley In related news, Union Farm Workers (UFW) posted a statement on X late Tuesday sounding the alarm on human cases of H5N1 in Yakima Valley dairy workers. The union said cases are being widely underreported in part because workers are often immigrants, and there is an ongoing US Immigration and Customs Enforcement (ICE) surge in Yakima Valley. “UFW staff are trying to support sick workers, but there are many barriers,” the post read. “Workers with milder symptoms are reluctant to be tested or miss work because sick cattle still need care. Workers are very afraid of ICE-related retaliation if they don’t follow employers’ instructions.” Workers are very afraid of ICE-related retaliation if they don’t follow employers’ instructions Earlier this month, a Yakima County resident with underlying health condition died from H5N1, and two others were reported as infected. All case-patients had occupational exposure to dairy cows. The death marked the only US H5N1 death recorded this year and the third recorded since 2024. According to the Centers for Disease Control and Prevention, there have been 74 human cases of H5N1, including three deaths, in the United States since 2024. The Union said many more workers are sick and are only being offered goggles and gloves for handling raw milk. “The workers report symptoms that range from mild upper respiratory infection with pinkeye to severe flu symptoms with bone pain, severe cough and high fever. We do not have reports that indicate human to human transmission, just workplace exposure to infected animals and milk,” the Union said. In the last 30 days, 51 cattle herds have been hit with H5N1, and in the past week, two herds in Idaho have been identified, as well as the Oregon herd. Washington state has had 43 detections since October 8. View five news elements
Time
Earlier this week, 2026-11-12.
Location and population
Oregon, United States; Yakima Valley, Washington, United States; dairy workers and cattle in the region.
Event
Oregon reported its first H5N1 case in cows, and Yakima Valley in Washington reported increased human H5N1 cases, with one death and two infections, according to the Union Farm Workers (UFW).
Scale, impact and risk
H5N1 has been detected in 22 states since 2024. In Yakima Valley, 51 cattle herds were affected in the last 30 days, and two herds in Idaho were identified in the past week. The USDA confirmed one death in Yakima County from H5N1 and two other infections, with occupational exposure to dairy cows noted. The risk to human health is considered low due to pasteurization, but the UFW claims underreporting due to fear of ICE retaliation.
Response, status and uncertainty
The USDA emphasized that the detection does not change its strategy and urged producers to report sick livestock. The UFW stated that workers are being offered only basic protective gear, and there are concerns about underreporting due to ICE-related fears. The CDC reported 74 human cases of H5N1 since 2024, including three deaths.

B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

禽流感病例随着迁徙而持续 - FarmWeekNow

Bird flu cases continue with migration - FarmWeekNow
新闻简报FarmWeekNow 于 2026 年 11 月 11 日发布的新闻报道强调了美国持续存在的禽流感病例,特别提到了 H5N1 到达印第安纳州的乳制品厂和全国各地持续存在的家禽病例。文章指出,这种情况随着迁徙而持续,表明病毒的传播可能与鸟类的迁徙有关。然而,该报告没有提供有关病例、死亡或暴露数量的具体细节,也没有提及任何正式的应对措施或风险评估。该信息作为 禽流感 当前状态的更新提供,除了对持续病例的一般观察之外,没有任何迹象表明确诊或疑似 暴发。这篇文章是有关农业主题的更广泛讨论的一部分,包括天气、收成和市场趋势,这可能意味着重点关注病毒对农业经营的影响,而不是公共卫生。内容来自农业新闻媒体 FarmWeekNow,由于缺乏有关 暴发 规模或风险的详细数据,该报告被认为是初步的。

时间
Rhiannon Branch 商品编辑 +3 教育 特色怀特塞德县校长被评为 IAITC 年度管理员 18 小时前 一般特色 11 月第一周零售化肥价格涨跌互现 19 小时前 最新电子版 FarmWeek 要查看我们最新的电子版,请单击左侧的图片。
地点与对象
美国,印第安纳州奶制品、家禽病例遍布全美
事件
据《FarmWeekNow》报道,随着 H5N1 病毒到达印第安纳州的乳制品厂,禽流感病例随着迁徙而继续,美国各地的家禽病例持续存在。
规模、影响与风险
所提供的证据中未报告。
应对、状态与不确定性
正在调查中,因为文章讨论了正在进行的禽流感病例,但没有提供应对措施的具体细节。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Bird flu cases continue with migration - FarmWeekNow

News Brief
The news report from FarmWeekNow, published on November 11, 2026, highlights the ongoing presence of bird flu cases in the United States, specifically mentioning H5N1 reaching Indiana dairies and poultry cases persisting across the country. The article notes that the situation is continuing with migration, suggesting the spread of the virus may be linked to bird movement. However, the report does not provide specific details on the number of cases, deaths, or exposures, nor does it mention any formal response measures or risk assessments. The information is presented as an update on the current status of avian influenza, with no indication of confirmed or suspected outbreaks beyond the general observation of continued cases. The article is part of a broader discussion on agricultural topics, including weather, harvest, and market trends, which may imply a focus on the impact of the virus on farming operations rather than public health. The content is sourced from FarmWeekNow, a media outlet covering agricultural news, and the report is considered preliminary due to the lack of detailed data on the scale or risk of the outbreak.

美国农业部确认俄勒冈州奶牛群中存在 高致病性禽流感 - Vet Candy

USDA Confirms Highly Pathogenic Avian Influenza in a Dairy Herd in Oregon - Vet Candy
新闻简报2026 年 11 月 11 日,美国农业部通过国家兽医服务实验室 (NVSL) 的 PCR 检测,确认俄勒冈州的一个奶牛群中存在 高致病性禽流感 (HPAI) H5 clade 2.3.4.4b。随着病毒传播到华盛顿州和印第安纳州等美国其他州,这标志着一个重大事件,引发了人们对生物安全和牲畜流动的担忧。一名华盛顿居民在接触奶牛后死于 H5N1 禽流感,凸显了该病毒对人类的潜在风险。美国农业部目前正在进行基因测序以最终确定结果。暴发 对兽医实践和动物健康管理具有影响,并持续努力监测和遏制传播。目前情况仍在调查中,随着基因分析完成,预计将有更多细节。
查看新闻五要素
时间
美国农业部于 2026 年 11 月 11 日确认了 暴发。证据中没有明确说明事件日期,但确认发生在该日期。
地点与对象
暴发 在美国俄勒冈州的一个奶牛群中得到证实。随着病毒的传播,受影响的人群包括俄勒冈州和其他州的奶牛。
事件
美国农业部证实俄勒冈州的奶牛群中存在 高致病性禽流感 (HPAI) H5 进化枝 2.3.4.4b。这是根据国家兽医服务实验室 (NVSL) PCR 检测证实的事件。
规模、影响与风险
随着 H5N1 禽流感蔓延到华盛顿州和印第安纳州等美国其他州,暴发 引起了人们对生物安全和牲畜流动的担忧。一名华盛顿居民因接触奶牛而死于 H5N1 禽流感。证据中没有完全量化影响的规模,但明确指出了风险。
应对、状态与不确定性
美国农业部正在进行基因测序以确定最终结果。应对措施包括对 暴发 进行持续调查和监测,这对生物安全和牲畜管理具有影响。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

USDA Confirms Highly Pathogenic Avian Influenza in a Dairy Herd in Oregon - Vet Candy

News Brief
The USDA confirmed the presence of highly pathogenic avian influenza (HPAI) H5 clade 2.3.4.4b in a dairy cattle herd in Oregon on November 11, 2026, through PCR detection by the National Veterinary Services Laboratories (NVSL). This marks a significant event as the virus spreads to additional U.S. states, including Washington and Indiana, raising concerns about biosecurity and livestock movement. A Washington resident has died from H5N1 bird flu following exposure to dairy cattle, highlighting the potential risk to humans. The USDA is currently conducting genetic sequencing to finalize the results. The outbreak has implications for veterinary practices and animal health management, with ongoing efforts to monitor and contain the spread. The situation remains under investigation, and further details are expected as the genetic analysis is completed.

首次在俄勒冈州奶牛中检测到 高致病性禽流感 - Morning Ag Clips

First Detection of Highly Pathogenic Avian Influenza in Oregon Dairy Cattle Confirmed - Morning Ag Clips
新闻简报2026 年 11 月 11 日,俄勒冈州农业部 (ODA) 确认首次在俄勒冈州奶牛中检测到 高致病性禽流感 (HPAI)。初步识别发生在 2026 年 11 月 6 日,通过俄勒冈州常规全州牛奶 监测 计划,俄勒冈州立大学 (OSU) 的俄勒冈兽医诊断实验室 (OVDL) 于 2026 年 11 月 10 日提供了确认。莫罗县受影响的乳制品已被隔离,以限制动物流动并实施疾病控制措施。目前尚未发现与此事件有关的人类病例,公共卫生官员强调,普通人群面临的风险仍然很低。巴氏灭菌牛奶和乳制品可以安全食用,因为病毒在巴氏灭菌过程中被灭活。ODA 正在与 USDA、OHA 和当地卫生当局合作监测和管理情况,确保对任何暴露的个人进行快速检测和 Z​​XQTERMUQXZ。此次检测突显了俄勒冈州乳制品 监测 计划(该计划于 2024 年 12 月开始)在及早识别 HPAI 和支持快速应对工作方面的有效性。
查看新闻五要素
时间
2026年11月6日报告初步结果;2026年11月10日确认;发布于 2026 年 11 月 11 日。
地点与对象
俄勒冈州,特别是莫罗县的奶牛。
事件
俄勒冈州立大学 (OSU) 的俄勒冈兽医诊断实验室 (OVDL) 于 2026 年 11 月 10 日首次在俄勒冈州奶牛中检测到 高致病性禽流感 (HPAI)。受影响的奶牛正在接受检疫。
规模、影响与风险
一只奶牛群受到影响;未发现人间病例。巴氏杀菌牛奶和乳制品仍然可以安全食用。一般人群的公共卫生风险较低。受感染的牛预计将在几周内康复。
应对、状态与不确定性
俄勒冈州农业部 (ODA) 启动了农场调查,并将样品提交给美国农业部确认。加强的生物安全措施已经到位。ODA 正在与美国农业部、俄勒冈州卫生局 (OHA) 和当地卫生官员进行协调。尚未发现人与人之间的 传播。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

First Detection of Highly Pathogenic Avian Influenza in Oregon Dairy Cattle Confirmed - Morning Ag Clips

News Brief
On November 11, 2026, the Oregon Department of Agriculture (ODA) confirmed the first detection of highly pathogenic avian influenza (HPAI) in Oregon dairy cattle. The initial identification occurred on November 6, 2026, through Oregon's routine statewide milk surveillance program, with confirmation provided by the Oregon Veterinary Diagnostic Laboratory (OVDL) at Oregon State University (OSU) on November 10, 2026. The affected dairy in Morrow County has been placed under quarantine to restrict animal movement and implement disease control measures. No human cases have been linked to this incident, and public health officials emphasize that the risk to the general population remains low. Pasteurized milk and dairy products are safe for consumption, as the virus is inactivated during pasteurization. ODA is working with USDA, OHA, and local health authorities to monitor and manage the situation, ensuring rapid testing and treatment for any exposed individuals. The detection highlights the effectiveness of Oregon's dairy surveillance program, which began in December 2024, in identifying HPAI early and supporting swift response efforts.

First Detection of Highly Pathogenic Avian Influenza in Oregon Dairy Cattle Confirmed Affected dairy under quarantine; milk and food supply remain safe for consumption PUBLISHED ON November 11, 2026 Preliminary detection of HPAI was identified through Oregon's routine statewide milk surveillance program, with preliminary results reported by the Oregon Veterinary Diagnostic Laboratory (OVDL) at Oregon State University (OSU) on November 6, 2026. (Photo by David Sjunnesson on Unsplash) SALEM, Ore. — Milk from a Morrow County dairy tested positive for highly pathogenic avian influenza (HPAI). This is the first detection of HPAI in Oregon dairy cattle. Preliminary detection of HPAI was identified through Oregon‘s routine statewide milk surveillance program, with preliminary results reported by the Oregon Veterinary Diagnostic Laboratory (OVDL) at Oregon State University (OSU) on November 6, 2026. The Oregon Department of Agriculture (ODA) immediately initiated an on-farm investigation and submitted additional samples to the United States Department of Agriculture (USDA) National Veterinary Services Laboratory (NVSL) for confirmation. Positive results were confirmed on November 10, 2026. Oregon’s routine statewide milk surveillance program identified the detection, demonstrating the effectiveness of the state’s ongoing monitoring efforts. No additional affected dairies have been identified at this time. Pasteurized milk and dairy products remain safe to consume, and there is no concern about the safety of the commercial milk supply. Pasteurization effectively inactivates the HPAI virus, and milk from sick animals is prohibited from entering the commercial milk supply. The U.S. Food and Drug Administration (FDA) continues to advise against consuming raw (unpasteurized) milk because it does not undergo the heating process that kills viruses and bacteria. “While this detection is significant, it is not unexpected given the continued spread of HPAI among dairy cattle nationally,” said Dr. Ryan Scholz, State Veterinarian for ODA. “Oregon‘s milk surveillance program worked exactly as intended, identifying this infection quickly and allowing us to respond rapidly with our state and federal partners. This detection demonstrates the value of the monitoring and preparedness efforts supported by Oregon‘s dairy industry and animal health partners since 2024.” ODA has placed the affected dairy under quarantine, and enhanced biosecurity measures are in place. The quarantine restricts animal movement and implements additional disease control measures while response activities are conducted. Response activities are focused on the impacted dairy and associated epidemiologic investigation. In most cases, infected dairy cows are expected to recover within a few weeks. ODA is coordinating closely with USDA, Oregon Health Authority (OHA) and Morrow County Public Health officials, OVDL, and Oregon‘s dairy industry partners as the investigation continues. Oregon‘s statewide dairy surveillance program, launched in December 2024, was established to detect HPAI early and support rapid response efforts. The program allows state and federal officials to implement disease control measures quickly when detections occur. Public health officials are providing education to farm workers exposed to infected animals and will monitor them for symptoms of avian influenza throughout the outbreak. “With the use of appropriate personal protective equipment, the risk to farm workers remains low. Our public health approach is focused on ensuring rapid testing and treatment for any individuals who develop symptoms to prevent severe disease,” said Melissa Sutton, M.D., medical director, respiratory viral pathogens, OHA. No human cases associated with this incident have been identified in Oregon. According to OHA and the Centers for Disease Control and Prevention (CDC), the current public health risk to the general population remains low. No cases of person-to-person transmission associated with this virus have been identified. HPAI was first detected in U.S. dairy cattle in March 2024 and has since been identified in multiple states. USDA provides current information on HPAI detections in livestock. Oregon began statewide dairy milk surveillance in December 2024 and continues to test milk from all licensed dairy operations routinely. ODA appreciates the cooperation of Oregon dairy producers and industry partners who participate in ongoing surveillance, disease reporting, and biosecurity efforts that help protect animal health and Oregon‘s dairy industry. Symptoms of HPAI in Dairy Cattle Symptoms can include: Decrease in feed consumption with a simultaneous decrease in rumination and rumen motility. Respiratory signs, including clear nasal discharge. Acute drop in milk production. Abnormal tacky or loose feces. Lethargy. Dehydration. Fever. Thicker, more concentrated, colostrum-like milk, or no milk production in severely affected animals. Some infected cattle may show no clinical signs. Most infected cattle recover within two to three weeks. Suspected cases of HPAI in any animal species should be reported immediately to ODA at 503-986-4711. For more information regarding HPAI in livestock, please visit the ODA HPAI webpage (http://oda.direct/AvianInfluenza). — Oregon Department of Agriculture Discover more dairy cattle Get Fresh Dairy dairy cows Spread the word RECOMMENDED ARTICLES THC Undetectable After Withdrawal Period in Cows Fed Hemp Byproduct June 30, 2025 CORVALLIS, Ore. – A new study examined feeding a hemp byproduct to cows and found that the trace amounts of psychoactive THC in hemp were undetectable in the milk and edible tissue of cows if they were weaned off the byproduct before milking or processing. On October 8, the National Veterinary Services Laboratories (NVSL) completed whole genome sequencing and confirmed that the virus is H5N1 genotype B3.13. Time
Preliminary results reported on November 6, 2026; confirmed on November 10, 2026; published on November 11, 2026.
Location and population
Oregon, specifically Morrow County dairy cattle.
Event
First detection of highly pathogenic avian influenza (HPAI) in Oregon dairy cattle confirmed by the Oregon Veterinary Diagnostic Laboratory (OVDL) at Oregon State University (OSU) on November 10, 2026. The affected dairy is under quarantine.
Scale, impact and risk
One dairy cattle herd affected; no human cases identified. Pasteurized milk and dairy products remain safe for consumption. Public health risk to the general population is low. Infected cattle are expected to recover within a few weeks.
Response, status and uncertainty
Oregon Department of Agriculture (ODA) initiated an on-farm investigation and submitted samples to USDA for confirmation. Enhanced biosecurity measures are in place. ODA is coordinating with USDA, Oregon Health Authority (OHA), and local health officials. No person-to-person transmission has been identified.

B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

Baloxavir 和 Molnupiravir 保护免疫抑制仓鼠免受严重 禽流感 - Bioengineer.org

Baloxavir and Molnupiravir Protect Immunosuppressed Hamsters Against Severe Avian Influenza - Bioengineer.org
新闻简报Bioengineer.org 于 2026 年 11 月 11 日发表的一项研究调查了 抗病毒药物 药物 baloxavir 和 molnupiravir 在保护免疫抑制仓鼠免受严重 禽流感 A(H5N1) 感染方面的有效性。该研究使用了从加拿大不列颠哥伦比亚省的人类病例中获得的病毒分离物,并针对用环磷酰胺治疗的免疫功能低下的仓鼠测试了这些药物。正常仓鼠没有表现出 死亡率 和完全的病毒清除,而免疫抑制仓鼠在没有 死亡率 的情况下具有 75% 的 死亡率 率。巴洛沙韦单药疗法可实现 100% 存活、减少病毒脱落并预防神经侵袭。高剂量molnupiravir还在肺和脑组织中实现了100%的存活率和病毒清除率。该研究强调了免疫功能低下个体患严重疾病的风险增加,并表明基于巴洛沙韦和莫努匹拉韦的治疗方案可能提供有效的治疗选择。这些发现基于实验模型,尚未适用于人类,尽管它们有助于大流行防范和 抗病毒药物 治疗研究。
查看新闻五要素
时间
该研究发表于 2026 年 11 月 11 日。实验是使用从加拿大不列颠哥伦比亚省的人类病例中获得的病毒分离物进行的,并于同一日期报告了结果。
地点与对象
该研究是在一个未指定的地点进行的,但研究中使用的病毒分离物是从加拿大不列颠哥伦比亚省的一个人类病例中获得的。该研究使用免疫抑制(用环磷酰胺处理)和正常的仓鼠作为实验模型。
事件
研究人员测试了 抗病毒药物 药物 baloxavir 和 molnupiravir 在保护免疫抑制仓鼠免受严重 禽流感 A(H5N1) 感染方面的功效。研究发现,这些药物显着减少了免疫功能低下的 死亡率 和病毒复制,而正常仓鼠则没有表现出 死亡率 和病毒完全清除。
规模、影响与风险
该研究报告称,在没有使用 死亡率 的免疫抑制仓鼠中,死亡率 的存活率为 75%,而巴洛沙韦单药治疗则实现了 100% 的存活。高剂量molnupiravir还可以预防死亡率并实现病毒清除。该研究强调了感染 禽流感 A(H5N1) 的免疫功能低下个体患严重疾病的风险,强调需要有效的 抗病毒药物 疗法。
应对、状态与不确定性
该研究是在受控实验环境中进行的,并在免疫抑制仓鼠上测试了 治疗 方案。这些发现基于实验结果,尚未适用于人类。研究小组纳入了安慰剂组进行比较,并评估了多种 抗病毒药物 方案。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Baloxavir and Molnupiravir Protect Immunosuppressed Hamsters Against Severe Avian Influenza - Bioengineer.org

News Brief
A study published on November 11, 2026, by Bioengineer.org investigated the effectiveness of antiviral drugs baloxavir and molnupiravir in protecting immunosuppressed hamsters from severe avian influenza A(H5N1) infection. The research used a viral isolate obtained from a human case in British Columbia, Canada, and tested these drugs against immunocompromised hamsters treated with cyclophosphamide. Normal hamsters showed no mortality and complete viral clearance, while immunosuppressed hamsters had a 75% mortality rate without treatment. Baloxavir monotherapy resulted in 100% survival, reduced viral shedding, and prevented neuroinvasion. High-dose molnupiravir also achieved 100% survival and viral clearance in both lung and brain tissues. The study highlights the heightened risk of severe disease in immunocompromised individuals and suggests that baloxavir and molnupiravir-based regimens may offer effective therapeutic options. These findings are based on experimental models and are not yet applicable to human populations, though they contribute to pandemic preparedness and antiviral therapy research.
Original source text (English)
HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER Friday, November 13, 2026 No Result View All Result Login HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs Lecturer PhD Studentship Postdoc Research Assistant EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs Lecturer PhD Studentship Postdoc Research Assistant EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER No Result View All Result No Result View All Result Home NEWS Science News Health Baloxavir and Molnupiravir Protect Immunosuppressed Hamsters Against Severe Avian Influenza by November 11, 2026 in Biology, Health Reading Time: 3 mins read 0 Baloxavir and Molnupiravir Protect Immunosuppressed Hamsters Against Severe Avian Influenza Share on FacebookShare on TwitterShare on LinkedinShare on RedditShare on Telegram Researchers investigating therapeutic options for avian influenza have found that the antiviral drugs baloxavir and high-dose molnupiravir effectively protected immunocompromised hamsters from lethal infection with a human isolate of influenza A(H5N1). Highly pathogenic avian influenza A(H5N1) viruses have continued to expand their geographic and animal host ranges, intensifying concerns regarding zoonotic transmission events and general pandemic preparedness. While immunocompromised individuals face elevated risks of developing severe, prolonged disease when infected by these emerging viruses, experimental evidence guiding clinical antiviral selection has remained scarce. To evaluate disease development and antiviral treatments, the research team characterized viral pathogenesis and assessed direct-contact transmission using an influenza A(H5N1) clade 2.3.4.4b genotype D1.1 virus strain. This specific viral isolate was recently obtained from an infected human case in British Columbia, Canada. Investigators established an animal model using hamsters immunosuppressed with cyclophosphamide and included normal hamsters to compare clinical infection outcomes, survival benchmarks, and viral shedding kinetics over the infection course. Discover more Engineering job board Research publication platform Scientific instrument sales The baseline experiments revealed clear contrasts between normal animals and immunosuppressed subjects. Normal hamsters experienced no mortality and successfully achieved complete viral clearance following exposure to the Canadian avian influenza isolate. In stark contrast, infection among the cyclophosphamide-treated immunosuppressed hamsters produced severe clinical illness marked by prolonged viral replication, viral invasion into the brain, and a 75 percent mortality rate. In addition, the research team detected no direct-contact viral transmission between the infected hamsters and uninfected contacts during these transmission evaluations. Discover more Get Cold Relief Check Symptoms Save on Rx Read Med Journals In subsequent therapeutic trials, investigators evaluated three distinct antiviral compounds administered as monotherapies or in dual combinations across the immunosuppressed cohort. Treatment protocols tested oseltamivir at 20 mg/kg twice daily for five days, baloxavir at 50 mg/kg given in two doses, and molnupiravir administered twice daily at either a low dose of 50 mg/kg or a high dose of 200 mg/kg. The team also evaluated a combination regimen pairing standard oseltamivir with low-dose molnupiravir, while maintaining an untreated placebo group for controlled comparison. The efficacy evaluations demonstrated striking differences across the therapeutic regimens compared with the placebo control, where infected hamsters exhibited 50 percent mortality. Baloxavir monotherapy resulted in 100 percent survival, shortened the duration of viral shedding, curtailed viral replication, lessened pulmonary inflammatory responses, and completely prevented neuroinvasion. High-dose molnupiravir at 200 mg/kg likewise prevented mortality and successfully achieved viral clearance in both lung and brain tissues. The combination of oseltamivir and low-dose molnupiravir yielded 75 percent survival, reduced morbidity relative to either monotherapy, and also accomplished viral clearance in both lung and brain tissues. By contrast, oseltamivir monotherapy produced 75 percent survival but failed to prevent severe clinical signs or reduce viral titers in target tissues, while low-dose molnupiravir monotherapy resulted in outcomes comparable to placebo. Together, these experimental findings indicate that human A(H5N1) isolates can cause severe and invasive pathology in immunocompromised hosts, while demonstrating that baloxavir and molnupiravir-based regimens offer effective therapeutic options. 中国确诊更多H9N2禽流感病例

More H9N2 avian flu cases confirmed in China
新闻简报中国新增两例H9N2禽流感病例,分别为广西壮族自治区一名9岁女童和辽宁省一名7岁男童。女孩于 10 月 13 日出现症状,男孩于 9 月 20 日发病。这些病例是更广泛趋势的一部分,因为中国大陆在过去六个月内记录了 23 例 H9N2 病例,其中除 6 例外,其余均为儿童。世界卫生组织(WHO)表示,H9N2病毒尚未获得持续人际传播的能力,进一步社区传播的风险仍然较低。CDC 确认了与受污染的苜蓿芽有关的大肠杆菌和沙门氏菌多州 暴发 感染的结束,该感染于 2026 年 7 月首次发现。暴发 涉及 77 例病例,其中 3 名患者感染两种病原体,10 名患者感染沙门氏菌,64 名患者感染大肠杆菌。16 个州报告了病例,六名患者住院,但没有死亡报告。CDC 指出,真实的患病人数可能高于报告的病例,并且 暴发 可能已经传播到已知的州之外。作为应对措施的一部分,苜蓿、萝卜芽和种子被召回。
查看新闻五要素
时间
中国新增两例H9N2禽流感病例,分别于10月13日和9月20日发病。CDC 于昨天宣布对与苜蓿芽有关的大肠杆菌和沙门氏菌疾病进行调查,并于 2026 年 7 月首次发现了 暴发。
地点与对象
这些病例发生在中国广西壮族自治区和辽宁省。过去六个月,中国大陆已记录 23 例 H9N2 病例,其中除 6 例外,其余均为儿童。
事件
世界卫生组织(WHO)报告了中国新增两例儿童 H9N2 禽流感病例。美国疾病控制和预防中心 (CDC) 确认,与受污染的苜蓿芽相关的多州大肠杆菌和沙门氏菌感染 暴发 事件已经结束。
规模、影响与风险
世界卫生组织指出,H9N2病毒尚未获得持续的人传人传播,进一步社区传播的风险仍然很低。
应对、状态与不确定性
CDC 宣布 暴发 结束,PulseNet 于 2026 年 7 月首次识别出 暴发。一些病例呈聚集性发生,其中明尼苏达州和威斯康星州报告的病例最多。真实的患病人数可能比报道的要多。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

More H9N2 avian flu cases confirmed in China

News Brief
In China, two new H9N2 avian flu cases were confirmed, involving a 9-year-old girl in Guangxi Zhuang autonomous region and a 7-year-old boy from Liaoning province. The girl showed symptoms on October 13, while the boy's onset was on September 20. These cases are part of a broader trend, as mainland China has recorded 23 H9N2 cases in the past six months, with all but six occurring in children. The World Health Organization (WHO) stated that H9N2 viruses have not acquired the ability for sustained human-to-human transmission, and the risk of further community-level spread remains low. The CDC confirmed the end of a multistate outbreak of E. coli and Salmonella infections linked to tainted alfalfa sprouts, which was first identified in July 2026. The outbreak involved 77 cases, with three patients infected with both pathogens, 10 with Salmonella, and 64 with E. coli. Cases were reported in 16 states, and six patients were hospitalized, though no deaths were reported. The CDC noted that the true number of sick people may be higher than the reported cases, and the outbreak could have spread beyond the known states. Alfalfa, radish sprouts, and seeds were recalled as part of the response.
Original source text (English)

CDC declares its probe into outbreak of E coli, Salmonella illnesses tied to alfalfa sprouts over News brief Stephanie Soucheray, MA Topics E coli Foodborne Disease Salmonella Jovan J / Flickr cc The Centers for Disease Control and Prevention (CDC) said yesterday that a multistate outbreak of Escherichia coli and Salmonella infections linked to tainted alfalfa sprouts is now over, after sickening at least 77 people. The case count represents one new infection since the most recent update on October 1. Of the 77 case-patients, three were sickened with both pathogens, 10 with Salmonella, and 64 with E coli. Cases were reported in 16 states, and six patients were hospitalized for their illnesses. No deaths were reported. Illness onset ranged from May 31 to October 16 of this year. Alfalfa, radish sprouts and seeds recalled “The true number of sick people in this outbreak was likely much higher than the number reported, and this outbreak may not have been limited to the states with known illnesses,” the CDC said. PulseNet first identified this outbreak in July 2026, and in August, Everything Sprouts, LLC recalled certain lots of sprouts sold under the Calco and Everything Sprouts brands at retailers and grocery stores, according to the CDC. In addition to alfalfa sprouts, radish sprouts and alfalfa sprout seeds were also eventually recalled. Some cases occurred in clusters, with Minnesota and Wisconsin reporting the most cases with 29 and 27, respectively. B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

斯诺霍米什县的死鸟检测呈禽流感阳性;官员称公共风险较低 - KOMO

Dead birds in Snohomish County test positive for bird flu; officials say low public risk - KOMO
新闻简报据斯诺霍米什县卫生局称,在华盛顿州斯诺霍米什县,门罗泰伊湖公园发现的死鸟初步检测出 高致病性禽流感 (HPAI)(也称为禽流感)呈阳性。该病毒在鸟类中自然传播,可导致鸡、火鸡、鹅和鸭的严重疾病和高死亡率。卫生官员强调,由于 传播 对人类的影响并不常见,因此公众面临的风险较低。然而,直接接触受感染鸟类的个人,例如后院鸡群的主人或处理病鸟或死鸟的人,风险较高。华盛顿鱼类和野生动物部门的工作人员按照机构和兽医的指导,穿着适当的个人防护装备收集了这些鸟类。他们将自我监测 禽流感 可能的症状。卫生部门表示,没有证据表明存在更广泛的社区风险,并且不知道该县有任何其他高致病性禽流感病例。官员们敦促人们报告家禽鸡群的疾病迹象,并建议不要让食腐鸟接触尸体,以防止病毒传播。
查看新闻五要素
时间
2026 年 11 月 9 日星期一下午 5:24;更新于 2026 年 11 月 9 日星期一晚上 10:01。
地点与对象
华盛顿州斯诺霍米什县;门罗的泰伊湖公园;鸡、火鸡、鹅和鸭。
事件
据斯诺霍米什县卫生部门称,在门罗泰伊湖公园发现的死鸟初步检测出 高致病性禽流感 (HPAI) 呈阳性,这种病毒也称为禽流感。
规模、影响与风险
卫生部表示,没有证据表明存在更广泛的社区风险,并且不知道斯诺霍米什县有任何其他 HPAI 病例。对公众的风险较低,传播 对人类的风险并不常见。直接接触受感染鸟类的人,例如后院鸡群的主人或处理病鸟或死鸟的人,面临更高的风险。
应对、状态与不确定性
华盛顿鱼类和野生动物部门的工作人员按照机构和兽医的指导,穿着适当的个人防护装备收集死鸟。他们会自我监测症状。卫生部敦促人们报告家养鸡群的疾病迹象,并建议不要让食腐鸟接触尸体。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Dead birds in Snohomish County test positive for bird flu; officials say low public risk - KOMO

News Brief
In Snohomish County, Washington, dead birds found at Lake Tye Park in Monroe have preliminarily tested positive for Highly Pathogenic Avian Influenza (HPAI), also known as bird flu, according to the Snohomish County Health Department. The virus circulates naturally among birds and can cause severe illness and high death rates in chickens, turkeys, geese, and ducks. Health officials emphasized that the risk to the general public is low, as transmission to humans is uncommon. However, individuals with direct exposure to infected birds, such as backyard flock owners or those handling sick or dead birds, are at higher risk. The Washington Department of Fish and Wildlife workers collected the birds wearing appropriate personal protective equipment, following agency and veterinary guidance. They will self-monitor for possible symptoms of avian influenza. The Health Department stated there is no evidence of broader community risk and is not aware of any other HPAI cases in the county. Officials urged people to report signs of illness in domestic flocks and advised against allowing scavenger birds to access carcasses to prevent the spread of the virus.
Original source text (English)

Health officials said the virus circulates naturally among birds and can cause severe illness and high death rates in chickens, turkey, geese and ducks.
