The Health Department said two workers from the Washington Department of Fish and Wildlife picked up dead birds at Lake Tye wearing appropriate personal protective equipment, following agency and veterinary guidance and standing guidance from health officials. At this time, the Health Department said there is no evidence of broader community risk, and it is not aware of any other cases of HPAI in Snohomish County. The Washington State Department of Health has information on its website about what to do if you see birds suspected of being ill and how to protect yourself. The state also tracks detections on the Washington State Avian Influenza Outbreak Map. Health officials urged people to report signs of illness in domestic flocks. If a flock experiences sudden death or illness of multiple birds, people should call the Washington State Department of Agriculture Sick Bird Hotline at (800) 606-3056 or use the agency’s online reporting tool. If birds have died, officials said to double-bag the carcasses and keep them in a cooler with ice until WSDA can arrange for sampling. They also advised people not to allow scavenger birds to access carcasses because it can spread the virus. 家禽养殖场确诊禽流感 - BBC

Bird flu confirmed at poultry premises - BBC
新闻简报英国诺福克郡德辛厄姆附近的商业家禽场发现一例禽流感(H5N1)确诊病例。这标志着今年秋天诺福克首次出现该病毒,使英国的病例总数达到五例。动植物卫生局(APHA)确认了暴发,现场所有家禽都将被人道扑杀。现场周围建立了 1.86 英里(3 公里)的保护区和 6.21 英里(10 公里)的 监测 区域。英国卫生安全局(UKHSA)表示,公共卫生风险仍然非常低,没有持续的人传人 传播 报告。正确煮熟的家禽和鸡蛋被认为可以安全食用。鸟类饲养员必须遵守严格的生物安全措施。该应对措施基于已证实的证据和官方指南,没有迹象表明存在更广泛的公共卫生风险。
查看新闻五要素
时间
该病例已于3天前确诊,公布日期为2026年11月10日。
地点与对象
暴发 在英国诺福克得到确认,特别是在德辛厄姆附近的商业家禽养殖场。
事件
动植物卫生局 (APHA) 已确认诺福克郡德辛厄姆附近的商业家禽中存在禽流感 (H5N1)。
规模、影响与风险
这是诺福克今秋首例禽流感病例,使英国病例总数达到五例。对公众健康的风险非常低,并且没有出现持续的人传人传播。正确煮熟的家禽和家禽产品可以安全食用。
应对、状态与不确定性
场内所有家禽都将被人道扑杀。已建立了 1.86 英里(3 公里)的保护区和 6.21 英里(10 公里)的 监测 区域。鸟类饲养者必须遵守严格的生物安全措施。应对措施基于确诊病例和官方指南。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Bird flu confirmed at poultry premises - BBC

News Brief
A confirmed case of bird flu (H5N1) has been identified at commercial poultry premises near Dersingham in Norfolk, UK. This marks the first occurrence of the virus in Norfolk this autumn, raising the total number of cases in the UK to five. The Animal and Plant Health Agency (APHA) confirmed the outbreak, and all poultry on the site will be humanely culled. A 1.86-mile (3km) protection zone and a 6.21-mile (10km) surveillance zone have been established around the site. The UK Health Security Agency (UKHSA) stated that the risk to public health remains very low, with no sustained human-to-human transmission reported. Properly cooked poultry and eggs are considered safe for consumption. Bird keepers are required to follow strict biosecurity measures. The response is based on confirmed evidence and official guidelines, with no indication of broader public health risks.
Original source text (English)

The Animal and Plant Health Agency (APHA) said highly pathogenic avian influenza H5N1 had been discovered in commercial poultry at premises near Dersingham. A 1.86-mile (3km) protection zone and a 6.21-mile (10km) surveillance zone have been put in place around the site. All poultry on the premises will be humanely culled, APHA said. The restrictions mean all bird keepers must follow stringent biosecurity measures in addition to the protection and surveillance measures. The number of cases of bird flu in the UK so far this autumn now stands at five. According to APHA, the protective zone means there are restrictions on the movement of birds and eggs and restrictions on people coming into contact with poultry and poultry eggs. Transmission from birds to humans is rare, and there has been no sustained human-to-human transmission. According to the UK Health Security Agency (UKHSA), the risk to the general public's health posed by avian influenza remained very low, while the Food Standards Agency (FSA) said it posed a very low food safety risk, with properly cooked poultry and poultry products, including eggs, safe to eat. You cannot vaccinate poultry or most captive birds against bird flu in England. APHA Do you have a story suggestion for Norfolk? Contact us below. 国家农业部董事就禽流感 疫苗 问题向美国农业部施压 - Capital Press

State ag directors press USDA for bird flu vaccine for cattle - Capital Press
新闻简报州农业主管敦促美国农业部制定针对牛群的禽流感疫苗,因为该病毒已在爱达荷州家禽养殖场中被确认,并且疑似传播至华盛顿州的奶牛群。美国农业部动物植物健康检验局确认了爱达荷州的暴发,影响了约10,000只鸟类。该病毒被认为已传播至华盛顿州的奶牛群,引发了对进一步传播的担忧。美国农业部尚未为牛群提供疫苗,且该情况仍在调查中。文章于2026年11月9日发表,突显了农业部门对禽流感威胁日益增长的紧迫应对需求。
查看新闻五要素
时间
文章于2026-11-09发表。爱达荷州的禽流感暴发于2026年11月5日被确认,美国农业部关于牛群可能将病毒带入华盛顿州奶牛群的声明是在2026年10月作出的。
地点与对象
爱达荷州古丁县和华盛顿州西部报告了禽流感 暴发。受影响的人群包括这些地区的家禽养殖场和奶牛场。
事件
州农业主管敦促美国农业部为牛开发禽流感 疫苗,因为该病毒已在爱达荷州家禽养殖场中得到证实,并怀疑已传播到华盛顿奶牛群。美国农业部动植物卫生检验局确认爱达荷州爆发禽流感 暴发。
规模、影响与风险
爱达荷州的禽流感暴发影响了约10,000只禽类,并怀疑该病毒传播到了华盛顿州的奶牛群。美国农业部尚未为牛提供 疫苗,进一步传播的风险是乳制品行业关注的问题。
应对、状态与不确定性
美国农业部正受到各州农业主管的压力,要求应对牛群感染禽流感 疫苗。爱达荷州的 暴发 已得到证实,正在调查向华盛顿奶牛群的传播情况。响应状态正在进行中,尚未确认可用的 疫苗。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

State ag directors press USDA for bird flu vaccine for cattle - Capital Press

News Brief
State agricultural directors are urging the USDA to develop a bird flu vaccine for cattle, as the virus has been confirmed in Idaho poultry operations and is suspected to have spread to Washington dairy herds. The outbreak in Idaho, confirmed by USDA’s Animal and Plant Health Inspection Service, affected about 10,000 birds. The virus is believed to have spread to Washington dairy herds, raising concerns about further transmission. The USDA has not yet provided a vaccine for cattle, and the situation remains under investigation. The article was published on November 9, 2026, highlighting the growing urgency for a targeted response to the bird flu threat in the agricultural sector.

B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

俄勒冈州农业官员确认该州首例奶牛感染禽流感病例

Oregon agriculture officials confirm state’s first case of bird flu in dairy cattle
新闻简报俄勒冈州农业官员证实该州首例奶牛感染禽流感病例,是在莫罗县的一个商业农场发现的。这种病毒被称为 高致病性禽流感,是通过该州的常规牛奶 监测 计划发现的,该计划于 2024 年底开始测试。这是俄勒冈州首例影响奶牛的禽流感病例,尽管该病毒自 2024 年 3 月以来一直在美国各地传播,影响家禽和其他动物。虽然俄勒冈州尚未报告有人死亡,但华盛顿亚基马县的一名农场工人最近死于禽流感,不过这种病例很少见。卫生官员强调,由于巴氏灭菌,牛奶供应仍然安全,受感染的牛奶被转移或销毁。俄勒冈州农业部已隔离受影响的农场,并正在调查 暴发,重点是监测暴露的农场工人,并确保在出现症状时进行快速检测和 Z​​XQTERMUQXZ。一般人群的公共卫生风险较低,但与奶牛或家禽密切接触的人面临较高的风险。

时间
美国俄勒冈州农业部于2026年11月10日星期二确认了该病例。该病毒最早于2024年3月在奶牛中被检测到,并已传播至多个州。相关内容于2026年11月12日发布。
地点与对象
该病例在俄勒冈州莫罗县得到证实,涉及奶牛。俄勒冈州的商业鸡和后院鸡中也检测到了该病毒,自 2022 年以来已有超过 100 万只鸡受到感染。该州的牛奶 监测 计划于 2024 年底开始测试。
事件
俄勒冈州农业官员证实该州首例奶牛感染禽流感病例。该病毒名为 高致病性禽流感,是在莫罗县的一个商业奶牛场中检测到的。这是俄勒冈州首次在奶牛中发现禽流感。
规模、影响与风险
该病例是在俄勒冈州的一个商业奶牛场发现的。No human deaths or severe illnesses have been reported in Oregon,though a farmworker in Washington’s Yakima County died from bird flu earlier this month.一般人群的公共卫生风险仍然较低,但与奶牛或家禽密切接触的个人面临较高的风险。来自受感染动物的牛奶被转移或销毁,以防止食品供应受到污染。
应对、状态与不确定性
The Oregon Department of Agriculture has placed the Morrow County farm under quarantine and is investigating the source of the infection. Officials are monitoring farmworkers exposed to the infected animals and will continue testing for symptoms.该州的牛奶 监测 计划正在按预期运行,并且农场工人在配备适当的防护设备的情况下面临的风险被认为很低。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Oregon agriculture officials confirm state’s first case of bird flu in dairy cattle

News Brief
Oregon agriculture officials confirmed the state’s first case of bird flu in dairy cattle, detected on a commercial farm in Morrow County. The virus, known as highly pathogenic avian influenza, was identified through the state’s routine milk surveillance program, which began testing in late 2024. This marks the first instance of bird flu affecting dairy cattle in Oregon, though the virus has been spreading across the U.S. since March 2024, impacting poultry and other animals. While no human deaths have been reported in Oregon, a farmworker in Washington’s Yakima County recently died from bird flu, though such cases are rare. Health officials emphasize that the milk supply remains safe due to pasteurization, and infected milk is diverted or destroyed. The Oregon Department of Agriculture has quarantined the affected farm and is investigating the outbreak, with a focus on monitoring exposed farmworkers and ensuring rapid testing and treatment if symptoms arise. The public health risk to the general population is low, but those working closely with dairy cattle or poultry are at higher risk.
Original source text (English)
Independent. Member-supported. For the Pacific Northwest. Independent. Member-supported. donate now Oregon agriculture officials confirm state’s first case of bird flu in dairy cattle By Alejandro Figueroa (OPB) Portland, Ore. Nov. 12, 2026 11:03 p.m. Up until now, bird flu’s effect on agriculture in the state has mostly impacted commercial and backyard chickens. For the first time, bird flu has been detected on a commercial dairy farm in Oregon. On Tuesday, the Oregon Department of Agriculture confirmed a positive test result for the virus, known as highly pathogenic avian influenza, at a Morrow County dairy. State agriculture officials have not identified any other affected dairies as of Thursday. THANKS TO OUR SPONSOR: Become a Sponsor Oregon agriculture officials say they’ve put the Morrow County farm under quarantine and will continue to investigate how the cows got sick. FILE - A cow stands in a field at the U.S. Department of Agriculture's National Animal Disease Center research facility in Ames, Iowa, on Tuesday, Aug. 6, 2024. Charlie Neibergall / AP Earlier this month, a farmworker in Washington’s Yakima County died after contracting the virus, though human deaths linked to bird flu are very rare. Health officials in that state say that the dairy worker had underlying health conditions. Two Yakima farmworkers also contracted the virus but did not experience severe illness, nor were they hospitalized. Officials maintain the nation’s milk supply and other dairy products are safe to consume since pasteurization effectively inactivates the virus. Milk from sick animals is also diverted or destroyed so that it does not enter the food supply. The current public health risk to the general population remains low, according to the Centers for Disease Control and Prevention, although people working in close contact with dairy cattle or poultry are at a higher risk of getting sick. While the Oregon detection is significant, it’s not surprising, said Dr. Ryan Scholz, the state veterinarian for the Oregon Department of Agriculture. The virus is spreading among dairy cattle across the country, he said. THANKS TO OUR SPONSOR: Become a Sponsor “Oregon’s milk surveillance program worked exactly as intended, identifying this infection quickly and allowing us to respond rapidly with our state and federal partners,” Scholz said in a statement. Bird flu was first detected in dairy cattle in March 2024 and has since been identified in multiple states, including Washington, Idaho and California. Oregon officials first discovered the virus in Morrow County through the state Department of Agriculture’s routine statewide milk surveillance program, which began weekly testing in late 2024. Oregon health officials did not immediately disclose how many people were exposed to the sick cows in Morrow County. But they are monitoring farmworkers exposed to the infected animals and will keep checking them for symptoms, said Dr. Melissa Sutton, the medical director of respiratory viral pathogens at the Oregon Health Authority. “With the use of appropriate personal protective equipment, the risk to farm workers remains low,” Sutton said in a statement. “Our public health approach is focused on ensuring rapid testing and treatment for any individuals who develop symptoms to prevent severe disease.” In Washington, the virus has spread rapidly with at least 41 dairy farms testing positive for bird flu over the last 30 days. This is the same virus that has affected millions of poultry birds across the U.S. since 2022, including more than one million commercial and backyard chickens in Oregon, according to the U.S. Department of Agriculture. Of all known bird flu infections in Oregon, most have been on commercial poultry farms and backyard flocks. There is evidence that suggests the virus affecting dairy cattle is mainly spreading from cow to cow, unlike in commercial or backyard poultry farms. Poultry usually catch the virus from wild migratory waterfowl. Unlike what the name suggests, avian flu can jump to other animals, including wild mammals. In Oregon the virus has been detected in at least one pig at a noncommercial farm, in skunks, raccoons, rodents and at least five cats last year. 伍斯特县发现 禽流感 病例 - 47abc - WMDT

Case of avian influenza detected in Worcester County - 47abc - WMDT
新闻简报2026年11月11日,马里兰州农业部(MDA)宣布在马里兰州沃斯特县一家商业肉鸡养殖场初步检测到高致病性H5N1 禽流感病毒。该病毒也被称为‘禽流感’,可通过鼻分泌物、唾液和粪便在鸟类中迅速传播,并通过野生鸟类、感染禽类或污染设备在禽群之间传播。因此,受影响的场所已被隔离,该禽群正在被扑杀以防止进一步扩散。受影响禽群的鸟类将不会进入食品供应链。MDA的国家兽医服务实验室正在进行检测以确认该疾病,最终结果预计很快公布。官员们强调保持严格生物安全措施和及时报告任何疾病迹象的重要性,以保护马里兰州的农业社区。对一般公众而言,传播的风险仍然较低,MDA正与州和联邦合作伙伴密切监测这一情况。

马里兰州伍斯特县。The affected population includes poultry at a commercial broiler operation and potentially 野生鸟类 species.

规模、影响与风险
受影响的财产已被隔离,并且正在减少鸡群的数量,以防止进一步传播。该病毒影响家禽和 野生鸟类,但 传播 对公众的风险仍然很低。
应对、状态与不确定性
农业部国家兽医服务实验室目前正在测试以确认该疾病。官员们敦促商业种植者和后院鸡群所有者维持严格的生物安全协议,并立即报告任何疾病迹象。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Case of avian influenza detected in Worcester County - 47abc - WMDT

News Brief
On November 11, 2026, the Maryland Department of Agriculture (MDA) announced the preliminary detection of highly pathogenic H5N1 avian influenza at a commercial broiler operation in Worcester County, Maryland. The virus, also known as 'bird flu,' spreads rapidly among birds through nasal secretions, saliva, and manure, and can travel between flocks via wild birds, infected poultry, or contaminated equipment. As a result, the affected property has been placed under quarantine, and the flock is being depopulated to prevent further spread. Birds from the affected flock will not enter the food supply chain. The MDA’s National Veterinary Services Laboratories are conducting tests to confirm the disease, with final results expected soon. Officials emphasize the importance of maintaining strict biosecurity protocols and promptly reporting any signs of illness to protect Maryland’s agricultural community. The risk of transmission to the general public remains low, and the MDA is collaborating with state and federal partners to monitor the situation closely.

Apps and Podcasts Community Calendar Meet The Team Contests Jobs What’s On Case of avian influenza detected in Worcester County November 11, 2026 Sophia Peters, WORCESTER COUNTY, Md. – The Maryland Department of Agriculture (MDA) has announced the detection highly pathogenic H5N1 avian influenza in Worcester County. Officials say, following routine surveillance, poultry at the affected commercial broiler operation preliminarily tested positive for the virus, also known as “bird flu”. As a result, the affected property has been placed under quarantine. The flock is being depopulated to prevent further spread of the disease, and birds from the affected flock will not enter the food supply chain. The U.S. Department of Agriculture’s National Veterinary Services Laboratories is currently testing to confirm the disease, with final results expected in the coming days. “It is critical that all commercial growers and backyard flock owners maintain strict biosecurity protocols and report any signs of illness immediately,” said MDA Secretary Kevin Atticks. “Our department, along with our state and federal partners, is monitoring the situation closely to protect Maryland’s agricultural community.” Avian influenza is a highly contagious respiratory virus that spreads rapidly among birds through nasal secretions, saliva and manure. The virus can easily travel between flocks via wild birds, contact with infected poultry, or on contaminated equipment, clothing and footwear. It affects domestic poultry including chickens, ducks and turkeys, as well as wild bird species such as geese, shorebirds and raptors. Risk of transmission to the general public remains low. For more information on avian influenza, visit MDA’s website here. H5N1 从伊利诺伊州后院的羊群传染给猫和老鼠 - AGDAILY

H5N1 jumps from Illinois backyard flock to cat and rats - AGDAILY
新闻简报2025 年 3 月,高致病性禽流感 (H5N1) 的 暴发 几乎消灭了伊利诺伊州农民后院的 105 只鸟,其中 100 只消失。该农民向密苏里大学提交了五只鸟和一只老鼠的样本进行测试。伊利诺伊大学厄巴纳-香槟分校兽医诊断实验室和密苏里大学兽医医学诊断实验室随后的分析证实,这只猫和另一只老鼠感染了与鸡相同的H5N1亚型。组织检测显示,所有 12 份猫样本和 15 份大鼠样本中的 11 份均被感染,在老鼠的尿液中也发现了病毒 RNA。该病毒携带 突变,增强其感染哺乳动物的能力,表明 跨物种溢出 的潜力。研究结果发表在疾病预防控制中心的期刊《新发传染病》上,强调了啮齿类动物作为病毒在 野生鸟类、家禽和其他哺乳动物之间传播的桥梁的风险。暴发 强调了生物安全措施的重要性,例如让家禽远离野生水禽、控制啮齿动物以及防止农场猫吃死鸟。调查仍在进行中,该报告警告农民对潜在的病毒 传播 风险保持警惕。
查看新闻五要素
时间
该事件发生于2025年3月,新闻报道发布日期为2026年11月12日。
地点与对象
据报道,伊利诺伊州发生了 暴发 事件,具体涉及农场后院的一群鸟、一只猫和野鼠。
事件
高致病性禽流感 (H5N1) 的 暴发 几乎摧毁了伊利诺伊州一位农民后院的羊群,随后被证实感染了一只猫和野鼠。该分析由伊利诺伊大学厄巴纳-香槟分校兽医诊断实验室和密苏里大学兽医医学诊断实验室进行。
规模、影响与风险
暴发 导致后院 105 只鸟中的 100 只死亡。组织检测发现所有 12 份猫样本和 15 份大鼠样本中的 11 份均受到感染,在大鼠尿液中也检测到了病毒 RNA。该病毒显示出 突变 增强其感染哺乳动物的能力,表明 跨物种溢出 的潜力。
应对、状态与不确定性
调查正在进行中,调查结果发表在疾病预防控制中心杂志《新发传染病》上。该研究强调了采取生物安全措施的必要性,例如让家禽远离野生水禽、控制啮齿动物以及防止农场猫吃死鸟。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

H5N1 jumps from Illinois backyard flock to cat and rats - AGDAILY

News Brief
In March 2025, an outbreak of highly pathogenic avian influenza (H5N1) nearly destroyed an Illinois farmer’s backyard flock of 105 birds, with 100 lost. The farmer submitted samples of five birds and a rat to the University of Missouri for testing. Subsequent analysis by the University of Illinois Urbana-Champaign Veterinary Diagnostic Laboratory and the University of Missouri Veterinary Medical Diagnostic Laboratory confirmed that the cat and a second rat were infected with the same H5N1 subtype as the chicken. Tissue testing revealed all 12 cat samples and 11 of 15 rat samples were infected, with viral RNA also found in the rat’s urine. The virus carried a mutation that enhances its ability to infect mammals, suggesting potential for spillover. The findings were published in the CDC journal Emerging Infectious Diseases, highlighting the risk of rodents acting as a bridge for the virus to move between wild birds, poultry, and other mammals. The outbreak underscores the importance of biosecurity measures, such as keeping poultry away from wild waterfowl, controlling rodents, and preventing farm cats from scavenging dead birds. The investigation remains ongoing, and the report serves as a warning for farmers to remain vigilant about potential virus transmission risks.
Original source text (English)

Tennessee project offers answers By AGDAILY Contributors Read More Read Tickborne meat allergy poses challenges for farmers Read Penn State study finds most soil microbes stay dormant after rainfall Read 3 farm dogs compete for AFBF People’s Choice Pup honors Agriculture news Nov 12, 2026 Livestock Read AGDAILY H5N1 jumps from Illinois backyard flock to cat and rats An outbreak of highly pathogenic avian influenza that nearly destroyed an Illinois farmer’s backyard birds also infected a cat and wild rats on the farm, according to an analysis by the University of Illinois Urbana-Champaign Veterinary Diagnostic Laboratory and the University of Missouri Veterinary Medical Diagnostic Laboratory. The case points to rodents as a potentially overlooked bridge for the virus to move between wild birds, poultry, and other mammals. In March 2025, the farmer lost 100 of his 105 backyard birds and took the remains of five birds and a rat to the University of Missouri. The farm sat about two miles from a reservoir lake, positioning it for the virus to reach the flock from wild waterfowl. A sample from one chicken tested positive for avian influenza. Four days afterward, the farm’s primary veterinarian sent the carcasses of a cat and a second rat to the University of Illinois lab, as there had been reports of infections in cats at the time. Both mammals carried the same H5N1 subtype identified in the chicken. Tissue testing found all 12 cat samples infected, along with 11 of 15 rat samples, and viral RNA also turned up in the rat’s urine. “The cat was expected, but the rat is the surprise,” said Dr. Leyi Wang, who heads the virology section at the U. of I. Veterinary Diagnostic Laboratory and serves as a professor of veterinary clinical medicine. “Since rats live on nearly every farm despite biosecurity, they may be an overlooked link between wild birds, poultry and other mammals.”= Sequencing of the viral genomes from the chicken, cat, and rat showed nearly identical sequences, indicating spillover from the poultry to the two mammals. All three carried a mutation that better equips the avian virus to infect mammals. “Because the mammalian-adaptation marker was already present in the bird virus, crossing into mammals may not require new mutations,” Wang said. Wang urged flock owners to keep poultry away from wild waterfowl, report sudden die-offs, control rodents, and prevent farm cats from scavenging dead birds. The findings were published in the CDC journal Emerging Infectious Diseases. This article was prepared with the assistance of automated tools and human research and verified by our editorial team. Share Nov 12, 2026 Crops Read AGDAILY About 275 gallons of diesel stolen from field equipment Approximately 275 gallons of diesel fuel were allegedly stolen from a tractor and combine parked in a Blackford County, Indiana, field overnight, prompting the sheriff’s office to warn farmers about securing equipment left unattended during harvest. According to the Blackford County Sheriff’s Office, the theft was discovered Wednesday morning after someone reportedly drained fuel from both machines parked along East County Road 600 North near the 5200 block. Based on recent agricultural diesel prices of approximately $5.50 to $5.75 per gallon, the stolen fuel could be worth more than $1,500. Beyond the cost of replacing the fuel, thefts can leave farmers facing equipment downtime during an already busy harvest season. No suspects or arrests have been announced, and the investigation remains ongoing. The sheriff’s office is encouraging farmers to remain vigilant when leaving tractors, combines, and other equipment in fields overnight. Large fuel tanks and remote locations can make unattended machinery attractive targets for thieves. Farmers can help reduce the risk of fuel theft by using locking fuel caps, installing security or trail cameras, and parking equipment in visible or well-lit locations when possible. Investigators are asking anyone with security camera footage from the area to come forward. They are also interested in information about anyone who may have recently obtained an unusually large quantity of diesel fuel under suspicious circumstances. Anyone with information is asked to contact the Blackford County Sheriff’s Office at 765-348-0930. Share Nov 12, 2026 Read AGDAILY Reporters Tickborne meat allergy poses challenges for farmers The first peer-reviewed study examining alpha-gal syndrome among farmers and ranchers found that those living with the tickborne red meat allergy averaged three medical visits before receiving a diagnosis, with some reporting more than 10 visits. Published in the Journal of Agromedicine, the study surveyed 201 farmers and ranchers across 26 states, making it the first research of its kind to focus specifically on how the condition affects agricultural producers. Researchers identified four distinct symptom patterns and found that symptoms differed between men and women. General practitioners and allergists were responsible for most diagnoses, but patients experiencing severe symptoms typically required more medical visits. The findings highlight a growing concern for agricultural workers, whose daily exposure to pastures, livestock and wooded areas puts them at greater risk for tick bites. Alpha-gal syndrome is a potentially serious allergy associated with tick bites, particularly those from the lone star tick. The condition causes reactions to a sugar molecule found in most mammalian meat and certain animal-derived products, including some dairy products and medications. Unlike typical food allergies, symptoms may not appear until three to six hours after exposure, making it difficult for patients to connect their reactions to something they ate. Symptoms vary widely among farmers and ranchers Researchers identified four distinct symptom patterns among participants: severe, gastrointestinal, mild and allergy-related. The most frequently reported symptoms included abdominal cramping, diarrhea, itchy skin and nausea. Other participants experienced hives, rashes, difficulty breathing and swelling. Women were more likely to report gastrointestinal symptoms, while men were more frequently represented in the mild symptom group. More than 85 percent of respondents experienced symptoms daily or weekly, yet only about half initially suspected their symptoms were related to food. Despite working in environments where tick exposure is common, fewer than half of participants, 46.8 percent, were familiar with alpha-gal syndrome before their diagnosis. The survey also found that 77 percent recalled being bitten by a tick, while 87 percent reported experiencing multiple bites. Nearly all respondents, 95 percent, said they regularly checked themselves for ticks. Getting a diagnosis can be difficult Participants averaged 2.98 medical visits before receiving a diagnosis, although some reported 10 or more visits. Those experiencing primarily allergy-related symptoms received diagnoses more quickly, averaging 2.25 visits. Participants with severe symptoms averaged 4.03 visits. General practitioners and allergists accounted for most diagnoses, with each responsible for roughly one-third of cases. Researchers noted that additional appointments can be particularly burdensome for agricultural workers, who may already face long drives to medical facilities, limited access to specialists and difficulty leaving their operations. The study also referenced previous research finding that 42 percent of surveyed health care providers had never heard of alpha-gal syndrome, highlighting the continued need for education in rural medical communities. Image by BY-_-BY, Shutterstock The implications extend beyond personal health. Farmers and ranchers may face continued tick exposure through everyday activities such as checking livestock, repairing fences, managing pastures and working in wooded or brush-covered areas. Alpha-gal syndrome can also complicate dietary choices and potentially affect exposure to certain animal-derived products. Researchers emphasized that the condition’s wide range of symptoms makes awareness especially important. Patients experiencing gastrointestinal problems may not recognize the possibility of an allergic reaction, particularly when symptoms develop hours after eating. The study has limitations, including recruitment primarily through social media and reliance on self-reported diagnoses that were not independently verified. The findings do not establish how common alpha-gal syndrome is among agricultural workers, but they highlight the challenges facing those who develop the condition. As tick populations expand into new geographic areas, researchers say improving awareness among agricultural workers and rural health care providers will be important for recognizing symptoms and reducing delays in diagnosis. Share Nov 12, 2026 Crops Read AGDAILY Reporters Penn State study finds most soil microbes stay dormant after rainfall Rainfall may bring much-needed moisture to dryland soils, but new research from Penn State University suggests that most of the microorganisms living beneath the surface don’t immediately respond when water arrives. Researchers studying biological soil crusts, commonly called biocrusts, found that only a small fraction of bacteria became active within six hours of rainfall. The findings could help scientists develop more effective ways to restore fragile soils in arid and semi-arid regions. Biocrusts are communities of cyanobacteria, algae, lichens, mosses and other microorganisms that form a thin layer across the soil surface. These living crusts play an important role in dryland ecosystems, helping stabilize soil and supporting biological processes in environments where water is scarce. However, researchers warn that biocrust ecosystems are increasingly threatened, with projections suggesting they could decline by as much as 40 percent over the next 45 years. The study, published in The ISME Journal, examined how microbial communities respond when dry soil receives moisture after extended periods without rainfall. Researchers collected biocrust samples near Moab, Utah, in the Colorado Plateau, where annual precipitation averages less than 10 inches. The samples were transported to Penn State and studied under controlled conditions that simulated rainfall during both daytime and nighttime. Image by Meryll, Shutterstock Using molecular techniques to identify bacteria actively producing proteins, the team discovered that most microorganisms remained dormant even after moisture became available. More mature biocrusts had a greater proportion of active microbes than younger, less-developed crusts. The researchers also found that the most abundant bacteria weren’t necessarily the ones that responded to rainfall. Surprisingly, whether rainfall occurred during daylight or nighttime had little effect on microbial activity during the first six hours. “Because biocrusts are supported by photosynthetic microbes, we expected them to have a very different response when the rain fell at night when they could not do photosynthesis versus during the day,” said Raúl Román, the study’s first author. “But we did not find that.” The findings suggest that understanding which microorganisms are present in soil may be less important than identifying which ones become active when conditions change. “Our results suggest that across these communities there is a relatively conserved group of microbes that are especially good at rapidly responding when water becomes available,” said Estelle Couradeau, Penn State assistant professor of soils and environmental microbiology. “Simply knowing which microbes are present may not tell us how the soil will function after rainfall. If we are to effectively restore biocrusts, we need to know which microbes actually activate when precipitation occurs — especially as climate change alters the timing and size of rainfall events. Photo by Tanja Esser, Shutterstock What the findings mean for dryland restoration The research could have implications for soil restoration efforts in drought-prone regions, where biological soil crusts contribute to ecosystem stability. By identifying microorganisms that respond quickly to moisture, scientists hope to eventually cultivate beneficial microbes that could be used to help restore damaged biocrust communities. “Biocrust microbial communities are able to maintain metabolic activity when it’s wet and then turn to quiescence or dormancy when it’s dry for a long period,” Román said. “That’s a very important advantage that allows them to colonize these harsh, barren areas. But we think with more research, we can isolate microbes that are active, and then culture them, and use them for restoration.” The work remains in the research stage, and the study did not evaluate microbial restoration treatments in agricultural fields. Still, understanding how these organisms respond to rainfall could help researchers better predict how dryland ecosystems will function as precipitation patterns change. For regions facing longer droughts or increasingly unpredictable rainfall, the findings offer another piece of the puzzle in understanding how soil ecosystems survive under difficult conditions. View five news elements
Time
The event occurred in March 2025, with the publication date of the news report being November 12, 2026.
Location and population
The outbreak was reported in Illinois, specifically involving a backyard flock of birds, a cat, and wild rats on the farm.
Event
An outbreak of highly pathogenic avian influenza (H5N1) was confirmed to have infected a cat and wild rats after it nearly destroyed an Illinois farmer’s backyard flock. The analysis was conducted by the University of Illinois Urbana-Champaign Veterinary Diagnostic Laboratory and the University of Missouri Veterinary Medical Diagnostic Laboratory.
Scale, impact and risk
The outbreak resulted in the loss of 100 out of 105 backyard birds. Tissue testing found all 12 cat samples and 11 of 15 rat samples infected, with viral RNA also detected in the rat’s urine. The virus showed a mutation that enhances its ability to infect mammals, indicating potential for spillover.
Response, status and uncertainty
The investigation is ongoing, and the findings were published in the CDC journal Emerging Infectious Diseases. The study highlights the need for biosecurity measures, such as keeping poultry away from wild waterfowl, controlling rodents, and preventing farm cats from scavenging dead birds.

ODFW 报告威拉米特谷鸭和鹅中禽流感病例增加 - KTVZ

ODFW reports increase in bird flu cases among ducks and geese in Willamette Valley - KTVZ
新闻简报俄勒冈州鱼类和野生动物部 (ODFW) 报告,2026 年 11 月 9 日,威拉米特山谷的鸭子和鹅中 高致病性禽流感 (HPAI) 病例有所增加。暴发 仍在持续,其中嘎嘎鹅是受影响最严重的亚种。ODFW 正在收集和测试鸟类以监测病毒的传播,该病毒可能会迅速影响鸟类种群和其他潜在的野生动物。该机构建议公众避免接触病鸟或死鸟,让宠物远离它们,不要捕捉它们或将它们运送到康复中心,因为没有有效的 治疗。尽管对人类的风险仍然较低,但自 2024 年以来,美国已确诊 74 例 禽流感 人类病例,其中 3 例死亡。鼓励公众报告没有明显疾病迹象的死鸟群,但工作人员可能因数量过多而无法回电。ODFW 还建议在处理死鸟时戴上手套和口罩,并在将尸体放入垃圾桶之前用双层袋子装好。家禽,如鸡、火鸡和珍珠鸡,也应向俄勒冈州农业部报告。
查看新闻五要素
时间
俄勒冈州鱼类和野生动物部 (ODFW) 于 2026 年 11 月 9 日报告威拉米特谷的禽流感病例有所增加。事件日期没有明确说明,但 暴发 正在持续进行,有迹象表明在鸟类种群中迅速传播。
地点与对象
据报道,暴发 主要出现在俄勒冈州威拉米特谷,影响鸭子和鹅,特别是嘎嘎鹅亚种。受影响的种群包括 野生鸟类 和潜在的家禽。
事件
ODFW 报告称,威拉米特山谷的鸭子和鹅中 高致病性禽流感 (HPAI) 病例有所增加。该事件得到了 ODFW 的证实,该机构收集并测试了鸟类以监测 暴发。
规模、影响与风险
该病毒在鸟类中迅速传播,其中鹅受影响最严重。目前没有提供 野生鸟类 的具体病例或死亡人数,但自 2024 年以来,美国已确诊 74 例 禽流感 人类病例,其中 3 例死亡。根据疾病预防控制中心的说法,人们面临的风险仍然很低。
应对、状态与不确定性
ODFW 敦促公众避免接触病禽并让宠物远离它们。他们还建议不要捕获病鸟或将病鸟运送到野生动物康复中心。鼓励公众报告没有明显疾病迹象的死鸟群。然而,由于举报量很大,工作人员可能无法回电。该机构目前不需要额外的鸟类收藏来进行测试。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

ODFW reports increase in bird flu cases among ducks and geese in Willamette Valley - KTVZ

News Brief
The Oregon Department of Fish and Wildlife (ODFW) reported an increase in cases of highly pathogenic avian influenza (HPAI) among ducks and geese in the Willamette Valley on November 9, 2026. The outbreak is ongoing, with the Cackling goose being the most affected subspecies. ODFW is collecting and testing birds to monitor the spread of the virus, which can rapidly affect bird populations and potentially other wildlife. The agency advises the public to avoid contact with sick or dead birds, keep pets away, and not capture or transport them to rehabilitation centers, as there is no effective treatment. While the risk to humans remains low, there have been 74 confirmed human cases of avian influenza in the U.S. since 2024, including three deaths. The public is encouraged to report groups of dead birds without obvious signs of illness, though staff may not be able to return calls due to high volume. ODFW also recommends wearing gloves and a mask when disposing of dead birds and double-bagging the carcass before placing it in the trash. Domestic birds, such as chickens, turkeys, and guinea fowl, should also be reported to the Oregon Department of Agriculture.
Original source text (English)
Home Livestream KTVZ Livestream KTVZ Livestream | Special Events News Ask the Mayor: Bend Ask the Mayor: Redmond Central Oregon KTVZ.com Poll Local Video National & International Oregon Capital Chronicle Oregon Journalism Project Regional The Local Lens There’s Good News Local Alert Weather Fire Alert First Pitch Forecast FutureTrack Radar Local Alert Forecast Local Alert Weather Days LIVE Road Conditions School Closings & Delays Shifting Seasons Weather Watchers Sports Beyond The Score The Big Playback Prep Scoreboard Decision 2026 Election Results Interactive Election Map Voter Guide Voter Resources Community 21 Cares For Kids CO For Kids Community Links Firebusters Fraud Prevention Tips One Class At A Time Pay It Forward Contests Big Ol’ Fish Junior Forecaster Pay It Forward Pet Pics Sunrise Birthdays Weather Watchers Custom Minute OnPoint Community Credit Union Severson Solutions Visiting Angel’s Senior Minute Events Jobs Central Oregon Careers KTVZ Careers Now Hiring Lifestyle Entertainment Events Health Money Pets Technology Problem Solvers Pump Patrol Share Big Ol’ Fish Pictures & Videos Weather Watchers About Us Advertise with KTVZ Careers & Internships Closed Captioning Contact Download Our Apps EEO Public Filing Email Newsletters FCC Public File KTVZ News Team On-Air Status Receiving KTVZ TV Listings Skip to Content News Local Alert Weather Sports Decision 2026 Contests Events Share Bend 67° × Central Oregon Strong: Silver Rail Elementary School Reopens 1 of 1 Oregon-Northwest ODFW reports increase in bird flu cases among ducks and geese in Willamette Valley 0:00/ 0:55LIVEQualityAuto 1080p Audio Subtitle SpeedNormal By Kelsey Merison Published November 9, 2026 3:54 pm SALEM, Ore. (KTVZ) -- Reports of highly pathogenic avian influenza (HPAI) are increasing among ducks and geese migrating through Oregon, with the majority of sick and dead birds being reported in the Willamette Valley. The Oregon Department of Fish and Wildlife (ODFW) says the Cackling goose, a smaller subspecies of the Canada goose, appears to be the most heavily affected. ODFW is urging the public to avoid contact with sick birds and keep pets away from them. People are also advised not to capture or transport sick birds to wildlife rehabilitation centers, as there is no effective treatment for infected wild or domestic birds. The virus can spread rapidly among bird populations and potentially to other wildlife. Signs of avian influenza in birds Symptoms in wild waterfowl can include: Lethargy or inability to fly Erratic behavior or loss of coordination Cloudy eyes Swimming in circles Head shaking Some ducks may carry and spread the virus without showing symptoms. Birds that develop clinical signs typically die within 72 hours. Infected wild mammals may also appear sick, show neurological abnormalities or be found dead after consuming infected birds. How to report sick or dead birds ODFW says its Wildlife Health staff have been collecting and testing birds to monitor the outbreak. The agency does not need additional bird collections for testing at this time. However, the public is encouraged to report groups of dead birds that show no obvious signs of illness before death. Reports can be made by calling 866-968-2600 or emailing wildlife.health@odfw.oregon.gov. Due to the high volume of reports, staff may not be able to return calls. If disposing of a dead bird, ODFW recommends wearing disposable gloves and a mask and double-bagging the carcass before placing it in the trash. The public should also report sick or dead domestic birds, including chickens, turkeys and guinea fowl, to the Oregon Department of Agriculture at 503-986-4711 or 800-347-7028. Risk to people remains low According to the U.S. Centers for Disease Control and Prevention (CDC), the risk of avian influenza to people remains low. Since 2024, there have been 74 confirmed human cases of avian influenza in the United States, including three deaths. 英国和苏格兰对禽流感的担忧促使农民下令将家禽留在室内 - 《卫报》

Bird flu fears prompt orders for farmers to keep poultry inside in England and Scotland - The Guardian
新闻简报2026 年,英国政府命令英格兰和苏格兰的农民将家禽饲养在室内,因为破坏性禽流感病毒的风险增加,这一点在 keep 和 野生鸟类 中都得到了证实。这项措施遵循 2025 年的类似规定,覆盖整个英格兰、苏格兰和威尔士。自 2021 年以来,这种疾病已导致英国 野生鸟类 种群灾难性死亡,并已蔓延至全球范围异常广泛的物种,包括南象海豹。虽然人类已发现禽流感,2025 年 1 月 1 日至 8 月 4 日期间已有 11 人死亡,但对人类健康的风险仍然很低。政府还实施了强化的生物安全措施,例如立即向动植物卫生局报告鸡蛋产量的变化和疑似疾病。他们表示,正确煮熟的家禽和家禽产品可以安全食用,并且预计不会对消费者的季节性家禽供应产生影响。
查看新闻五要素
时间
继2025年禽流感风险增加和确诊人数不断增加后,英国政府于2026年发布了预防区声明。
地点与对象
这些限制适用于英格兰和苏格兰的家禽和圈养鸟类,并于 2025 年在英格兰、苏格兰和威尔士宣布设立预防区。
事件
由于破坏性禽流感病毒的风险增加,英国政府下令英格兰和苏格兰的农民将家禽饲养在室内,这一点已在 keep 和 野生鸟类 中得到证实。
规模、影响与风险
自 2021 年以来,这种疾病已导致英国 野生鸟类 种群灾难性死亡,并已蔓延至全球范围异常广泛的物种。它也在人类中被发现,2025 年 1 月 1 日至 8 月 4 日期间记录了 11 人死亡,但对人类健康的风险仍然非常低。
应对、状态与不确定性
政府已实施强化的生物安全措施,包括立即向动植物卫生局报告鸡蛋产量的变化和疑似疾病。连续第二年宣布设立预防区,政府预计不会对消费者获得季节性家禽产生影响。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Bird flu fears prompt orders for farmers to keep poultry inside in England and Scotland - The Guardian

News Brief
In 2026, the British government ordered farmers in England and Scotland to keep poultry inside due to an increased risk of a devastating strain of bird flu, which has been confirmed in both kept and wild birds. This measure follows a similar designation in 2025, which covered all of England, Scotland, and Wales. The disease has caused catastrophic die-offs in UK wild bird populations since 2021 and has spread to an unusually broad range of species globally, including southern elephant seals. While bird flu has been detected in humans, with 11 deaths recorded between January 1 and August 4, 2025, the risk to human health remains very low. The government has also implemented enhanced biosecurity measures, such as reporting changes in egg production and suspected disease immediately to the Animal and Plant Health Agency. They stated that properly cooked poultry and poultry products are safe to eat and do not anticipate an impact on seasonal poultry availability for consumers.

Photograph: Jim Holden/Alamy Bird flu fears prompt orders for farmers to keep poultry inside in England and Scotland Increased risk of devastating strain means restrictions imposed for second year in a row on those keeping birds Poultry and captive birds across England and Scotland must be kept inside for the second year in a row due to an increased risk of a devastating strain of bird flu. The British government has issued an England-wide avian influenza prevention zone, meaning individuals with 50 or more poultry or captive birds must house them inside. A similar designation was made across the whole of Scotland on Tuesday. Birdkeepers must also follow enhanced biosecurity measures including reporting changes in egg production and reporting suspected disease immediately to the Animal and Plant Health Agency. The government said the declaration followed an increased risk and escalating number of confirmations of the highly pathogenic avian influenza, or bird flu, in kept and wild birds. A prevention zone was also declared across all of England, Scotland and Wales in 2025. The disease has been spreading rapidly since 2014 and has devastated UK wild bird populations since the summer of 2021. It has also spread across an unusually broad range of species around the world, including in four of the five main southern elephant seal populations. In some south Atlantic colonies, more than 90% of newborn pups have died, alongside about half of all adult females. Bird flu has caused catastrophic die-offs – could a wave of extinctions follow? Read more The disease has also been detected in humans, with 11 deaths recorded globally between 1 January and 4 August 2025. This strain of bird flu causes illness and high death rates in birds, and flu-like symptoms and conjunctivitis in humans who come into close contact with infected animals. The British government said the current risk to human health from bird flu remained very low. The government says it does not anticipate an impact on the availability of Christmas turkeys or other seasonal poultry for consumers. The Food Safety Authority said properly cooked poultry and poultry products, including eggs, were safe to eat. The UK chief veterinary officer, Christine Middlemiss, said: “Having avian influenza confirmed in your flock can be devastating. B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

H5N1 在一个月内影响华盛顿 40 个奶牛群 - 今日乳业新闻

H5N1 affects 40 dairy herds in Washington in one month - Dairy News Today
新闻简报据地区农业出版物称,H5N1 禽流感 在一个月内影响了华盛顿州中部的 40 个奶牛群。亚基马县的一名农场工人死于美国第三起已知的 H5N1 死亡病例,凸显了食用生奶的风险。该病毒在受感染奶牛的乳腺组织中广泛复制,导致生牛奶中病毒浓度很高,在冷藏条件下,活的传染性病毒可以保留长达五周。美国儿科学会 (AAP) 敦促立法者禁止销售未经高温消毒的牛奶和奶酪,因为它们对儿童和免疫功能低下的人存在风险。FDA 和 USDA 认为在获得许可的设施中加工的商业乳制品是安全的。生物安全措施包括牛群 监测、挤奶设备消毒以及巴氏灭菌工艺验证。这些行动旨在减轻病毒的传播并保护公众健康。
查看新闻五要素
时间
事件日期为2026年11月1日,发布日期为2026年11月9日。报告影响的时间范围为1个月。
地点与对象
地点是美国华盛顿州中部。受影响的人群包括奶牛群和农场工人,特别是亚基马县的奶牛群和农场工人。
事件
一个多月以来,在华盛顿州中部的 40 个奶牛群中检测到了 H5N1 禽流感。据 Animals 24-7 和地区农业出版物报道,亚基马县的一名农场工人死于美国第三例 H5N1 致命病例。
规模、影响与风险
一个月内,华盛顿州中部有 40 头奶牛群被诊断出感染 H5N1。一名农场工人的死亡是美国第三起死亡病例。该病毒在乳腺组织中广泛复制,导致原奶中病毒浓度较高。原奶在冷藏条件下可保留活的传染性病毒长达五周,这对儿童和免疫功能低下的人构成风险。
应对、状态与不确定性
美国儿科学会 (AAP) 呼吁禁止零售未经高温消毒的牛奶和由其制成的奶酪。兽医监测和生物安全措施包括牛群监测、挤奶设备消毒、个人防护设备的使用以及巴氏灭菌工艺的验证。在获得许可的设施中加工的商业乳制品经 FDA 和 USDA 确认是安全的。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

H5N1 affects 40 dairy herds in Washington in one month - Dairy News Today

News Brief
H5N1 avian influenza has affected 40 dairy herds in central Washington state within one month, according to regional agricultural publications. A farmworker in Yakima County died from the third known fatal case of H5N1 in the United States, highlighting the risks of raw milk consumption. The virus replicates extensively in the mammary tissue of infected cows, resulting in high viral concentrations in raw milk, which can retain live, infectious virus for up to five weeks under refrigeration. The American Academy of Pediatrics (AAP) has urged lawmakers to ban the sale of unpasteurized milk and cheeses due to the risks to children and immunocompromised individuals. Commercial dairy products processed in licensed facilities are deemed safe by the FDA and USDA. Biosecurity measures include herd surveillance, disinfection of milking equipment, and validation of pasteurization processes. These actions aim to mitigate the spread of the virus and protect public health.

Corporate M&A Companies Coops Appointments and Resignations Interview Events MENA Dairy Congress Upcoming events Presentations Photoreports Advertising H5N1 affects 40 dairy herds in Washington in one month United States 09.11.2026 Subscribe to weekly digest Source: en.edairynews.com 593 The detection of avian influenza on dairy farms in central Washington coincides with renewed warnings about raw milk consumption. The American Academy of Pediatrics (AAP) has renewed its call for a ban on its sale following the death of a farmworker in Yakima County. The worker died on November 1, 2026, in the third fatal H5N1 case recorded in the United States, according to information compiled by Animals 24-7 and regional agricultural publications. The veterinary monitoring cited puts the number of cattle herds diagnosed over the past month in central Washington state at 40, amid new autumn outbreaks among birds and mammals. The warning from the American Academy of Pediatrics (AAP), addressed to state and federal lawmakers, calls for explicit bans on the retail sale and distribution of unpasteurized milk and cheeses made from it. The organization highlights the risks to children and immunocompromised people. The sale of raw fluid milk remains legal in 30 of the country's 50 states. The medical recommendations note that H5N1 replicates extensively in the mammary tissue of infected cows, resulting in high viral concentrations in raw milk. The studies cited indicate that raw milk can retain live, infectious virus for up to five weeks under conventional refrigeration. This persistence is among the reasons given for strengthening health surveillance of dairy products that have not undergone heat treatment. Regarding industrial processing, continuous-flow high-temperature, short-time (HTST) pasteurization protocols completely inactivate H5N1. The information also identifies ultra-high-temperature (UHT) treatment as effective against the virus and cites confirmation from the Food and Drug Administration (FDA) and the United States Department of Agriculture (USDA) that commercial dairy products processed in licensed facilities are safe. Poultry outbreaks have led to the disease-control culling of 94,200 commercial laying hens in Oregon and 1.3 million birds in Stanislaus County, California. Outside the United States, outbreaks have been recorded at 27 sites in Canada, along with outbreaks affecting more than 225,000 birds in Yucatán, Mexico. These incidents coincide with the virus circulating among North American dairy herds. In Salt Lake County, Utah, H5N1 was detected for the first time at a captive mink breeding facility, where mortality approached 80%. The agricultural sector continues to monitor potential pathways for viral mutation. The biosecurity measures described include herd surveillance, disinfection of milking equipment between groups of animals, the use of personal protective equipment by workers in milking parlors, and validation of pasteurization processes at dairy plants. Location and population
The location is central Washington state, USA. The affected population includes dairy herds and farmworkers, particularly those in Yakima County.
Event
H5N1 avian influenza was detected in 40 dairy herds in central Washington state over one month. A farmworker in Yakima County died from the third fatal H5N1 case in the United States, reported by Animals 24-7 and regional agricultural publications.
Scale, impact and risk
Forty dairy herds were diagnosed with H5N1 in central Washington state in one month. The death of a farmworker marks the third fatal case in the U.S. The virus replicates extensively in mammary tissue, leading to high viral concentrations in raw milk. Raw milk can retain live, infectious virus for up to five weeks under refrigeration, posing risks to children and immunocompromised individuals.
Response, status and uncertainty
The American Academy of Pediatrics (AAP) has called for a ban on the retail sale of unpasteurized milk and cheeses made from it. The veterinary monitoring and biosecurity measures include herd surveillance, disinfection of milking equipment, use of personal protective equipment, and validation of pasteurization processes. Commercial dairy products processed in licensed facilities are confirmed safe by the FDA and USDA.

The impact on dairy cattle is a new concern, though no specific case or death counts are provided in the evidence. The risk associated with this case is not explicitly stated, but it underscores the potential for bird flu to spread beyond its usual hosts. The response includes official communication and monitoring efforts, with no mention of isolation, contact tracing, or treatment measures. The evidence is sourced from OPB, which is a reputable media outlet, and the information is considered reliable based on the confirmation by state officials.

海鸟是受 禽流感 暴发 打击最严重的动物 - RSPB

Seabirds amongst hardest hit by avian influenza outbreaks - RSPB
新闻简报一项由RSPB进行的新研究指出,包括海鸥、 terns、企鹅和鸭子在内的海鸟在2000年至2020年间受到了暴发的严重影响。研究人员分析了全球40起大规模死亡率事件的数据,发现大多数鸟类种群在暴发后立即下降了高达17%,一些物种在暴发后的监测期间未能恢复。报告指出,海鸟通常在密集的群体中筑巢,这有助于病毒的快速传播,而它们的长途觅食路线可能促进了病毒在不同群体之间的扩散。自2020年以来,暴发的严重程度有所增加,尤其是在繁殖季节,人们对这种疾病对猛禽和哑天鹅的持续影响表示担忧。RSPB呼吁继续监测并采取行动,以支持易受影响的种群并减轻未来高致病性禽流感(HPAI)带来的风险。
查看新闻五要素
时间
该研究分析了2000年至2020年间的暴发,并最新更新了2021年HPAI对英国的影响。当前报告于2026年11月11日发布。
地点与对象
该报告涵盖了全球鸟类种群,特别提到了英国海鸟和猛禽,包括疣鼻天鹅、白尾海雕、金雕、游隼和秃鹰。
事件
RSPB 进行的新研究发现,海鸟,如海鸥、燕鸥、企鹅和鸭子,在 2000 年至 2020 年 HPAI 暴发 期间经历了严重的种群数量损失。一些种群,如非洲企鹅、岬角鸬鹚、普通潜鸭、大黑背鸥和簇毛鸭,在 暴发 监测期间没有恢复。
规模、影响与风险
研究发现,在 HPAI 暴发 发生后,大多数鸟类种群数量立即下降了 17%,其中一些物种未能恢复。海鸟的长距离觅食路线及其密集群落的筑巢习惯增加了感染快速传播的风险。该报告还指出,自 2020 年以来,暴发 的严重程度有所增加,特别是在繁殖季节。
应对、状态与不确定性
皇家鸟类保护协会强调需要持续进行种群监测,以确定最易感染高致病性禽流感的物种。正在优先采取行动减少脆弱鸟类种群的额外压力并支持恢复工作。该报告基于 40 起大规模 死亡率 事件的数据,并且仍在持续进行中,未来可能会产生更严重的影响。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Seabirds amongst hardest hit by avian influenza outbreaks - RSPB

News Brief
A new study by the RSPB highlights that seabirds, including gulls, terns, penguins, and ducks, have been among the hardest hit by avian influenza outbreaks between 2000 and 2020. Researchers analyzed data from 40 global mass mortality events and found that most bird populations experienced a decline of up to 17% immediately after outbreaks, with some species failing to recover within the post-outbreak monitoring period. The report notes that seabirds often nest in dense colonies, facilitating rapid infection spread, and their long-distance feeding routes may contribute to the virus spreading between distant colonies. Since 2020, there has been an increase in outbreak severity, particularly during the breeding season, with concerns about the ongoing impact on birds of prey and Mute Swans. The RSPB is calling for continued monitoring and action to support vulnerable populations and mitigate future risks from HPAI.
Original source text (English)
About us Who we are How we are run How we are funded The RSPB’s Annual Report 2025–26 Our history What's happening Nature news Our priceless protected areas The UK's Bird of the Year 2026 Big Schools’ Birdwatch RSPB Winter Raffle 2026/27 Helping nature What we do Volunteer with us Business partnerships Support us What you can do Birds and wildlife Bird A-Z Identifying birds and wildlife Feeding birds near you Declines, disease and dangers – the issues facing birds Who should I call if I am concerned about a bird? How to report crimes against wild birds Days out Visit a reserve Find an event near you Search Shop Membership Donate Search Menu Home Whats happening Back to News Bird flu impacts global review News Seabirds amongst hardest hit by avian influenza outbreaks New research has identified which groups experienced the biggest losses from bird flu outbreaks between 2000 and 2020. 11th Nov 20265 min read Mute Swan Outbreaks of Highly Pathogenic Avian Influenza (HPAI) can cause large population losses across a wide range of bird species. By combining data from 40 different mass mortality events across the world, new research shows that some bird groups, such as gulls, terns and other seabirds, appear more likely to suffer significant population losses, than others, such as waterfowl. The long-term impact of HPAI HPAI is a contagious, and potentially deadly, viral infection which spreads between birds through saliva, nasal secretions, droppings, predation and scavenging. Where outbreaks occur, thousands of birds can die, causing an initial drop in population size. Researchers at the RSPB collated datasets from across the world where bird populations were monitored before, during and after HPAI outbreaks which occurred pre-2020. They found that most populations experienced a decline immediately following outbreaks, with an average 17% drop in number of birds. In most cases, populations recovered following this initial impact and bounced back within a few years. However, in 25% of cases, populations did not recover from these initial declines within the post-outbreak monitoring period (which varied between 4-17 years). Vulnerable seabirds among most impacted Gulls, terns, penguins and ducks, experienced some of the strongest initial declines after disease outbreaks. Populations of African Penguin, Cape Cormorant, Common Pochard, Great Black-backed Gull, Peregrine Falcon, and Tufted Duck failed to recover following HPAI outbreaks. Seabirds often nest in dense colonies, living closely alongside each other, making it easy for infections to spread quickly between individuals. Their long-distance feeding routes mean it is also possible for infections to spread between distant colonies. The long-lived nature of many seabirds also means the initial impacts of mass mortality events may not become apparent until there is a shortfall in breeding age adults. However, there was also evidence of resilience in some affected populations. Waterfowl species (ducks, geese and swans) appeared to suffer less short-term population impacts. These species tend to raise large numbers of young and therefore may be able to bounce back quicker following a mortality incident. There is also evidence of natural immunity spreading rapidly amongst some affected waterfowl populations. However post-2020 we have seen an increase in the size and severity of outbreaks in these species. Common Pochard The future of HPAI Since 2020, we have seen an escalation in the impacts from the virus globally with even more species and countries affected by HPAI, and the virus now even detected on Gough Island, one of the most remote inhabited islands in the world. There has also been an increase in the severity of outbreaks occurring during the breeding season. This means as monitoring continues, we may see different and potentially more severe population impacts from post-2020 outbreaks. Since the current strain reached the UK in 2021, the greatest impacts in UK birds have been seen in seabirds, but we’re increasingly concerned about the ongoing impact on birds of prey. There is evidence of reduced breeding success in both White-tailed Eagles and Golden Eagles, with local population declines observed in Peregrine and Buzzard. The consistently high numbers of HPAI cases being seen in Mute Swans over a number of years is also worrying. Continued population monitoring is essential for making sure we are able to identify species most vulnerable to the impacts of HPAI. Action to reduce additional stressors on vulnerable wild bird populations and identifying ways we can support the recovery of threatened populations will be key to protecting our most vulnerable species through future HPAI outbreaks. This work was funded by our RSPB Avian Flu Appeal, which directly supports research and action addressing the threat of HPAI to our wild birds. Thank you so much to everyone who supported this appeal. You can now help our scientists undertake vital research so we can save our wildlife by donating to our Wildlife Disease Fund. Support our bird disease research here. Share this article Facebook Bluesky Send via Email Copy Link
View five news elements
Time
The study analyzed outbreaks between 2000 and 2020, with the latest update on the impact of HPAI in the UK in 2021. The current report was published on 2026-11-11.
Location and population
The report covers global bird populations, with specific mention of UK seabirds and birds of prey, including Mute Swans, White-tailed Eagles, Golden Eagles, Peregrine Falcons, and Buzzards.
Event
New research conducted by the RSPB identified that seabirds, such as gulls, terns, penguins, and ducks, experienced significant population losses during HPAI outbreaks between 2000 and 2020. Some populations, like African Penguins, Cape Cormorants, Common Pochards, Great Black-backed Gulls, and Tufted Ducks, did not recover within the post-outbreak monitoring period.
Scale, impact and risk
The study found that most bird populations experienced a decline of up to 17% immediately after HPAI outbreaks, with some species failing to recover. The long-distance feeding routes of seabirds and their nesting habits in dense colonies increase the risk of rapid infection spread. The report also notes an increase in outbreak severity since 2020, particularly during the breeding season.
Response, status and uncertainty
The RSPB is emphasizing the need for continued population monitoring to identify species most vulnerable to HPAI. Action to reduce additional stressors on vulnerable bird populations and support recovery efforts is being prioritized. The report is based on data from 40 mass mortality events and is ongoing, with potential for more severe impacts in the future.

尤卡坦半岛的 H5N1 暴发 使家禽业进入高度戒备状态 - 墨西哥商业新闻

H5N1 Outbreak in Yucatan Puts Poultry Sector on High Alert - Mexico Business News
新闻简报2026 年 11 月 10 日,墨西哥尤卡坦半岛乌曼的一个商业蛋鸡养殖场确认了 H5N1 禽流感 暴发。世界动物卫生组织 (WOAH) 根据墨西哥农业当局的数据报告了这一事件。在实施遏制措施之前,暴发 导致 862 人自然死亡。包括 SENASICA 及其中央动物健康委员会 (CPA) 在内的联邦和地区卫生当局迅速执行了相关协议,例如建立 10 公里的风险范围并减少剩余 224,998 只鸟类的数量,以防止进一步的 传播。该事件引发了对整个家禽行业生物安全框架和风险管理策略的紧急重新评估,凸显了该行业的脆弱性。暴发 还给零售和商业食品服务运营带来了物流挑战并加剧了成本推动型通货膨胀。这一事件凸显了墨西哥家禽生产和供应链持续面临的风险。
查看新闻五要素
时间
暴发于2026年11月10日确认,活动日期与确认日期相同。发布日期也是2026年11月10日。
地点与对象
暴发 事件发生在墨西哥尤卡坦半岛乌曼市。它涉及大规模、集约化的商业鸡群,共 225,860 只蛋鸡。
事件
H5N1 禽流感 暴发 在墨西哥尤卡坦半岛乌曼的一个商业蛋鸡养殖场得到确认。世界动物卫生组织(WOAH)根据墨西哥农业当局的数据报告了这一事件。
规模、影响与风险
在实施遏制措施之前,暴发 导致 862 人自然死亡。它对家禽业造成了严重破坏,促使人们重新评估生物安全框架和风险管理策略。该活动还给零售和商业食品服务业务带来了物流摩擦,并加剧了成本推动型通货膨胀。
应对、状态与不确定性
联邦和地区卫生当局,包括 SENASICA 及其中央动物健康委员会 (CPA),执行了强制性遏制协议。其中包括建立 10 公里的风险范围并减少剩余 224,998 只鸟类的数量。该回应基于 WOAH 和墨西哥当局确认的数据。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

H5N1 Outbreak in Yucatan Puts Poultry Sector on High Alert - Mexico Business News

News Brief
An H5N1 avian influenza outbreak was confirmed on a commercial laying-hen farm in Uman, Yucatan, Mexico, on November 10, 2026. The World Organisation for Animal Health (WOAH) reported the event based on data from Mexican agricultural authorities. The outbreak resulted in 862 natural mortalities before containment measures were implemented. Federal and regional sanitary authorities, including SENASICA and its Central Commission for Animal Health (CPA), swiftly executed protocols such as establishing a 10km risk perimeter and depopulating the remaining 224,998 birds to prevent further transmission. The incident has triggered urgent reevaluation of biosecurity frameworks and risk management strategies across the poultry sector, highlighting vulnerabilities in the industry. The outbreak has also introduced logistical challenges and intensified cost-push inflation in both retail and commercial food service operations. This event underscores the ongoing risks to Mexico's poultry production and supply chains.
Original source text (English)

WhatsAppLinkedInTwitterFacebookEmailShare By MBN Staff MBN staff Tue, 11/10/2026 12:04 3 min read DIA READ ARTICLE Ready to read 0:00 1x Following the confirmation of an H5N1 avian influenza outbreak in Yucatan, Mexico’s animal protein sector faces an urgent biosecurity test. Federal authorities and commercial operators are rapidly executing containment protocols, reevaluating risk frameworks, and addressing potential supply chain disruptions across domestic agricultural markets. ___ Mexico’s animal protein sector is confronting a biosecurity test following the official confirmation of a highly pathogenic H5N1 avian influenza outbreak on a commercial laying-hen farm located in the Yucatan Peninsula. This animal health event was formally reported by the World Organisation for Animal Health (WOAH) based on comprehensive epidemiological data submitted directly by Mexican agricultural authorities. According to these official filings, the virus was positively identified within a large-scale, intensive commercial flock comprising 225,860 laying hens situated specifically in the municipality of Uman. Prior to veterinary intervention and the implementation of emergency sanitary measures, the affected flock recorded 862 natural mortalities. In response to the viral detection, federal and regional sanitary authorities, —notably SENASICA and its specialized Central Commission for Animal Health (CPA) branch, swiftly executed mandatory containment protocols. These emergency measures included the immediate establishment of a strict 10km risk perimeter surrounding the index premises alongside the total depopulation of the remaining 224,998 birds to prevent any further lateral transmission. The unfolding details of this sanitary operation were extensively documented via The Poultry Site report. Marking Mexico’s first confirmed commercial poultry outbreak since May 2024, the incident has immediately reverberated across domestic protein supply chains, prompting agricultural conglomerates, major poultry integrators, and B2B supply chain directors to urgently reevaluate their internal biosecurity frameworks, risk management strategies, and contingency distribution plans. The reemergence of the H5N1 virus underscores the persistent and structural vulnerabilities confronting intensive industrial poultry production corridors across the country. While Mexican sanitary authorities and leading protein producers have consistently maintained heightened surveillance networks following past global epizootic waves, localized viral incursions continue to pose severe and unpredictable threats to regional production capacity, table egg output volumes, and wholesale price stability. As previously explored in analytical assessments regarding Mexico's Agrifood Sector Navigates Market Changes, ongoing structural adjustments, mounting climate pressures, and evolving regulatory mandates are continuously reshaping domestic agricultural corridors. For major national market participants such as Industrias Bachoco and Pilgrim’s Pride México, the timing of the Yucatan outbreak compounds an already complex and multifaceted array of macroeconomic headwinds. The broader Mexican agrifood landscape in late 2026 exhibits a striking structural dichotomy, defined by robust foreign direct investment inflows running parallel to severe primary production challenges, as detailed further in Mexico's Food Industry Faces Staple Shortages and Trade Shifts. Animal protein producers are already actively navigating fluctuating feed grain acquisition costs, stringent environmental compliance standards, and shifting consumer purchasing habits set against the backdrop of broader inflationary pressures. The introduction of an acute avian influenza event adds substantial logistical friction, threatening regional table egg distribution networks and intensifying cost-push inflation across both retail channels and commercial food service operations. View five news elements
Time
The outbreak was confirmed on 2026-11-10, with the event date being the same as the confirmation date. The publication date is also 2026-11-10.
Location and population
The outbreak occurred in the municipality of Uman, Yucatan Peninsula, Mexico. It involved a large-scale, intensive commercial flock of 225,860 laying hens.
Event
An H5N1 avian influenza outbreak was confirmed on a commercial laying-hen farm in Uman, Yucatan, Mexico. The event was reported by the World Organisation for Animal Health (WOAH) based on data from Mexican agricultural authorities.
Scale, impact and risk
The outbreak resulted in 862 natural mortalities before containment measures were implemented. It has caused significant disruptions to the poultry sector, prompting reevaluation of biosecurity frameworks and risk management strategies. The event has also introduced logistical friction and intensified cost-push inflation across retail and commercial food service operations.
Response, status and uncertainty
Federal and regional sanitary authorities, including SENASICA and its Central Commission for Animal Health (CPA), executed mandatory containment protocols. These included establishing a 10km risk perimeter and depopulating the remaining 224,998 birds. The response is based on confirmed data from WOAH and Mexican authorities.

俄勒冈州野生动物官员报告威拉米特山谷感染禽流感的水禽数量不断增加 - NPR for Oregonians

Oregon wildlife officials report growing numbers of waterfowl with bird flu across the Willamette Valley - NPR for Oregonians
新闻简报据美国国家公共广播电台俄勒冈州新闻报道,俄勒冈州野生动物官员报告称,威拉米特山谷感染禽流感的水禽数量有所增加。该报告于 2026 年 11 月 13 日发布,强调了人们日益增长的担忧,但没有提供具体的病例数、死亡人数或暴露人数。它还没有详细说明确切的应对措施或调查状态。地方当局正在跟踪情况,但需要进一步的信息来评估 暴发 的全面规模和风险。这份报告对禽流感在野生动物中潜在传播的可能性发出了警报,这可能对公共健康和动物福利产生影响。

B routine priorityPublic-health news

Oregon wildlife officials report growing numbers of waterfowl with bird flu across the Willamette Valley - NPR for Oregonians

News Brief
Oregon wildlife officials have reported an increase in the number of waterfowl infected with bird flu across the Willamette Valley, according to a news report from NPR for Oregonians. The report, published on November 13, 2026, highlights the growing concern but does not provide specific case counts, death tolls, or exposure numbers. It also does not detail the exact response measures or investigation status. The situation is being tracked by local authorities, but further information is needed to assess the full scale and risk of the outbreak. This report serves as an alert about the potential spread of bird flu among wildlife, which could have implications for public health and animal welfare.

B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

亚基马县一人感染禽流感后死亡

One person dies after being infected with Bird Flu in Yakima County
新闻简报华盛顿州亚基马县的一份报告显示,一人在奶牛场工作时感染禽流感(禽流感)死亡。联合农场工人工会于 2026 年 11 月 12 日分享了这一信息,表示至少一名工人因接触该病毒而死亡。亚基马县确认有三人被感染,但对公众的风险被认为很低。工会还指出,还有更多工人被感染,症状从轻微到严重不等。尽管提供了防护装备,但工人们没有收到有关 疫苗接种 或流感检测的信息。目前情况正在调查中,证据中没有提供有关传播或遏制措施的进一步细节。
查看新闻五要素
时间
该报告于太平洋标准时间 2026 年 11 月 12 日上午 8:07 发布。证据中没有明确说明事件日期,但截至报告发布时,暴露发生在华盛顿州亚基马县。
地点与对象
该事件发生在华盛顿州亚基马县,涉及接触禽流感的奶牛场工人。受影响的人群包括接触过感染病毒的奶牛群的农场工人。
事件
一名人员在亚基马县的一家奶牛场工作时感染禽流感(禽流感)后死亡。联合农场工人工会报告了这一事件,亚基马县确认有三人被感染。
规模、影响与风险
已有三人感染禽流感,其中一人死亡。亚基马县表示,公众面临的风险较低。工会提到,还有更多工人被感染,症状从轻微到严重不等。
应对、状态与不确定性
联合农场工人工会报告称,为工人提供了手套、口罩和其他防护装备,但没有提及 疫苗接种 或流感检测。亚基马县已确认感染,但 暴发 的全部范围及其影响仍在调查中。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

One person dies after being infected with Bird Flu in Yakima County

News Brief
A report from Yakima County, Washington, indicates that one person has died after being infected with Bird Flu (Avian Flu) while working on a dairy farm. The United Farm Workers Union shared this information on November 12, 2026, stating that at least one worker had died following exposure to the virus. Yakima County confirmed three individuals have been infected, though the risk to the general public is considered low. The union also noted that many more workers are infected and experiencing symptoms ranging from mild to severe. Despite being provided with protective gear, workers are not receiving information on vaccinations or flu testing. The situation is under investigation, with no further details on the spread or containment measures provided in the evidence.

B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

农场工人对漏报的禽流感病例发出警报 - 新闻周刊

Farm Workers Sound Alarm on Underreported Bird Flu Numbers - Newsweek
新闻简报华盛顿州亚基马谷的农场工人对禽流感感染的漏报表示担忧。联合农场工人工会(UFW)声称,实际感染人数远高于官方报道的数字。暴发 涉及 H5 流感病毒,该病毒已感染了 30 多个奶牛群,并导致至少 1 人死亡。县官员称已有三人被感染,但 UFW 声称,由于工人担心 ICE 报复以及缺乏病假工资和个人防护装备培训,许多病例仍未得到检测。自 暴发 开始以来,CDC 已在美国记录了 70 多例人类感染病例,但尚未记录到人与人之间的 传播。公众面临的风险被认为很低,但农场工人由于接触受感染的动物而面临高风险。亚基马县卫生委员会对死者表示哀悼,并正在努力解决这一问题,而 UFW 则继续倡导为工人提供更好的保护。
查看新闻五要素
时间
文章发表于2026年11月11日。事件日期并未明确说明,但 暴发 正在进行中,并且自 2024 年以来一直有报道。
地点与对象
暴发 以华盛顿州亚基马谷为中心,影响农场工人和奶牛群。受影响的人群包括乳制品工人和接触受感染牛的居民。
事件
农场工人联合会(UFW)透露,华盛顿州感染禽流感 暴发 的人数远高于最初报告的人数。亚基马县卫生委员会报告称,一名居民因接触受感染的牛而感染禽流感后死亡。自 暴发 启动以来,CDC 已报告全美范围内发生 70 多起人类感染病例。
规模、影响与风险
暴发 已感染 30 多个奶牛群,并造成至少 1 人死亡。县官员声称有三人被感染,但 UFW 表示实际数字被大大低估了。普通民众面临的风险仍然很低,没有人与人之间 传播 的记录。然而,农场工人被认为是感染病毒风险最高的群体之一。
应对、状态与不确定性
UFW 正在尝试支持生病的工人,但面临一些障碍,例如担心 ICE 相关的报复以及缺乏病假工资和个人防护装备培训。亚基马县卫生委员会已确认一例死亡,并表示哀悼。CDC 报告了人类感染,但没有人传人的 传播。目前情况仍在调查中,我们正在联系 UFW 和县官员以征求进一步评论。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Farm Workers Sound Alarm on Underreported Bird Flu Numbers - Newsweek

News Brief
Farm workers in Yakima Valley, Washington State, have raised concerns about the underreporting of avian flu infections. A union, United Farm Workers (UFW), claims that the actual number of people infected is much higher than what has been officially reported. The outbreak involves the H5 influenza virus, which has infected over 30 dairy herds and led to at least one human death. County officials state three people have been infected, but the UFW asserts that many cases remain untested due to workers' fears of ICE retaliation and lack of sick pay and PPE training. The CDC has documented more than 70 human infections in the U.S. since the outbreak began, though no person-to-person transmission has been recorded. The risk to the general public is considered low, but farm workers are at high risk due to their exposure to infected animals. The Yakima County health board has expressed condolences for the deceased and is working to address the situation, while the UFW continues to advocate for better protections for workers.
Original source text (English)
U.S. News Politics AI Politics Tech Fact Check Sports Global Iran War Russia-Ukraine Middle East China And Asia Live Blog Better Planet All World News Lifestyle Family & Parenting Entertainment Travel Pets Automotive Relationships Science Health Personal Finance Better Workplaces Readers Choice Opinion My Turn All Opinion Rankings Health Finance Products Education Workplaces Corporate Responsibility All Rankings Watch The 1600 The Royal Report Unconventional From The Paddock Newsmakers Extra Newsletters Dare to Dream Events Essays Podcasts Vantage Insights Magazine CEO Circle Announcements Leadership Dev Pragad Subscribe Sign In U.S. NewsPoliticsAI PoliticsTechFact CheckSports Global Iran WarRussia-UkraineMiddle EastChina And AsiaLive BlogBetter PlanetAll World News Lifestyle Family & ParentingEntertainmentTravelPetsAutomotiveRelationshipsScienceHealthPersonal FinanceBetter WorkplacesReaders Choice Opinion My TurnAll Opinion Rankings HealthCorporate ResponsibilityFinanceProductsEducationWorkAll Rankings Watch The 1600The Royal ReportUnconventionalFrom The PaddockNewsmakers Social FacebookInstagramYouTubeRedditTikTokTwitter/X Extra NewslettersDare to DreamEventsEssaysPodcastsVantageInsightsMagazineCEO CircleAnnouncementsLeadershipDev Pragad The number of people infected by an outbreak of avian flu in Washington State is much higher than initially reported, a farm workers' union has revealed. An outbreak of the H5 influenza virus has spread rapidly throughout the Yakima Valley, infecting over 30 dairy herds and causing the death of at least one person. County officials claim three people have been infected with the virus following exposure at a dairy, but United Farm Workers (UFW) says the real numbers have been vastly underreported. “We have now connected with a number of workers and have confirmed that more workers are sick with avian flu symptoms who have not been tested,” the UFW said in a statement on X. “Their symptoms range from very mild to very severe. “The workers report symptoms that range from mild upper respiratory infection with pinkeye to severe flu symptoms with bone pain, severe cough and high fever. We do not have reports that indicate human to human transmission, just workplace exposure to infected animals and milk.” Newsweek has contacted the UFW and county health officials for additional comment. The UFW cites an ongoing ICE surge in Yakima Valley as part of the reason for the depressed figures, claiming their presence has made many workers reluctant to engage with public agencies and fearful of disobeying their employers. The lack of proper sick pay and PPE training has also helped spread the virus and encouraged workers with milder symptoms not to report the infection. “UFW staff are trying to support sick workers, but there are many barriers,” the union added. “Workers with milder symptoms are reluctant to be tested or miss work because sick cattle still need care. Workers are very afraid of ICE-related retaliation if they don’t follow employers instructions.” The Yakima County health board reported the death of a resident on Sunday after they contracted avian flu through exposure to infected cattle. The individual, who had underlying health conditions, was hospitalized after experiencing severe illness and died shortly afterwards. Their identity has not been revealed. “The Yakima health district offers its heartfelt condolences to the individual’s family, friends, and loved ones during this difficult time,” the health board said in a statement. Two other deaths have been linked to avian flu since it became a national public health concern in 2024. The CDC has reported more than 70 human infections throughout the U.S. since the start of the outbreak, although there have been no recorded instances of person-to-person transmission and the risk to the general populace remains low. Farm workers remain one of the groups with the highest risk of contracting the virus due to their exposure to infected cattle. “Protecting dairy workers protects everyone,” the UFW said. “As a reminder, consumers are not at any reasonable risk from consuming pasteurized milk. The front line of this risk, the human firewall, are the workers in the dairies (and the workers in the plants who process the raw fluid milk).” Contact Newsweek editors on this story: Ben Kelly and Cristina Diciu. 禽流感卷土重来,一如既往的致命。生奶工可能面临的风险尤其大。- 动物 24-7

Bird flu is back, deadly as ever. Raw milkers may be especially at risk. - Animals 24-7
新闻简报文章报道,H5N1 禽流感 卷土重来,并且致命,持续存在的 暴发 影响着养殖动物、野生动物和生奶工。据描述,2026 年西海岸禽流感大流行对包括太平洋西北地区和南极洲在内的多个地区造成了严重破坏,但没有提供具体病例或死亡人数。尽管没有确诊或疑似病例的详细信息,但重点是生奶工面临的风险。据报道,截至 2026 年 11 月 3 日,疫情已经开始蔓延,但在美国选举季并未成为一个突出问题。文章没有提及任何具体的应对措施或调查,导致事态悬而未决,信息有限。内容来源于动物相关问题的新闻媒体《Animals 24-7》,证据基于一般报道,没有详细数据。
查看新闻五要素
时间
2026 年西海岸禽流感大流行被描述为正在进行中,H5N1 禽流感 截至 2026 年 11 月 10 日处于活跃状态。事件日期没有明确说明,但值得注意的是,截至 2026 年 11 月 3 日,大流行正在发生。

据报道,2026 年西海岸禽流感大流行将影响从母鸡到水貂等养殖动物、从太平洋西北地区到南极洲的野生动物以及生奶工,这些人可能面临的风险尤其大。

应对、状态与不确定性
文章指出,禽流感大流行仍在持续,不会成为选举问题,但没有提及任何具体的应对措施或调查。该状态被描述为未解决,信息有限。

俄勒冈州成为第 22 个在奶牛中检测到禽流感的州 - 俄勒冈首都纪事报

Oregon becomes 22nd state to detect bird flu in dairy cattle - Oregon Capital Chronicle
新闻简报据《俄勒冈州首都纪事报》2026 年 11 月 12 日发表的一份报告称,俄勒冈州已成为第 22 个在其奶牛种群中检测到禽流感的州。该文章强调了人们对 禽流感 传播日益增长的担忧,这种病毒可能会产生人畜共患病的影响。虽然检测得到证实,但证据中并未详细说明影响的具体规模,包括病例数或死亡人数。该报告没有提供有关检测或接触者追踪等应对措施的进一步信息,表明情况仍在调查中。这一进展凸显了管理牲畜 禽流感 暴发 的持续挑战,这可能对动物和人类健康构成风险。
查看新闻五要素
时间
俄勒冈州首都纪事报 – 主页精选故事 参议员。
地点与对象
美国俄勒冈州;乳牛。
事件
据《俄勒冈州首都纪事报》报道,俄勒冈州已成为第 22 个在奶牛中发现禽流感的州。这是基于在该州奶牛群中检测到病毒而确认的事件。
规模、影响与风险
在奶牛中检测到禽流感表明人畜共患 传播 对动物健康和可能对人类健康构成潜在风险。然而,所提供的证据中并未报告具体的影响规模、病例数或死亡人数。
应对、状态与不确定性
《俄勒冈州首都纪事报》报道称,奶牛中发现了禽流感,但没有提供有关检测、隔离或接触者追踪等应对措施的进一步细节。调查情况仍在调查中。

Oregon becomes 22nd state to detect bird flu in dairy cattle - Oregon Capital Chronicle

News Brief
Oregon has become the 22nd state to detect bird flu in its dairy cattle population, according to a report by the Oregon Capital Chronicle published on November 12, 2026. The article highlights the growing concern over the spread of avian influenza, which can have zoonotic implications. While the detection is confirmed, the specific scale of impact, including case counts or deaths, is not detailed in the evidence. The report does not provide further information on the response measures, such as testing or contact tracing, indicating that the situation is still under investigation. This development underscores the ongoing challenge of managing avian influenza outbreaks in livestock, which may pose risks to both animal and human health.

禽流感导致野生动物死亡引发新的保护问题 - 自然世界新闻

Bird Flu's Wildlife Toll Raises New Conservation Questions - Nature World News
新闻简报《自然世界新闻》于 2026 年 11 月 12 日发表的题为《禽流感对野生动物造成的死亡引发了新的保护问题》的文章讨论了 H5N1 禽流感对野生动物的影响,特别是在巴布亚新几内亚,引发了人们对保护和灭绝风险的担忧。虽然这篇文章强调了 H5N1 对生态系统的更广泛影响,但它没有提供具体病例或死亡人数。相反,它侧重于野生动物健康和环境变化的更广泛背景,例如气候变化对海洋生物的影响以及真菌在恢复退化草原中的作用。文章还涉及其他相关主题,包括海洋热浪期间小丑鱼的行为以及人工智能在生物多样性研究中的潜力。不过,它没有详细说明与H5N1相关的任何具体应对措施或调查情况。该文章作为总体概述,而不是关于已确认的 暴发 或有针对性的干预措施的报告。

时间
首页 动物 生物学 环境 健康与医学 科技 旅行空间 Natureworldnews.com 让我们联系趋势气候变化 地球鲸 动物 海葵白化改变了小丑鱼在海洋热浪期间的行为 作者:Madz Dizon 来自巴布亚新几内亚的实地观察表明,海葵白化改变了小丑鱼的行为,增加了群体内的防御和攻击性。
地点与对象
巴布亚新几内亚、佛罗里达州、蒙古、北极、拉森火山国家公园、马绍尔群岛环礁、哈德逊河谷、南极洲的泰勒谷、犹他州、墨西哥。
事件
禽流感(H5N1)的死亡引发了新的保护问题,研究人员和牧场主正在争论其对野生动物和生态系统的影响。文章还讨论了其他相关主题,如气候变化、海洋热浪和生物多样性研究。
规模、影响与风险
该文章报道了 H5N1 病毒的死亡,引发了有关野生动物灭绝风险的问题,但没有提供具体病例、死亡或接触人数。它还提到了其他环境和生物学研究,但没有量化其影响。
应对、状态与不确定性
该文章只是一般性概述,并未具体说明与 H5N1 相关的任何调查、测试、隔离、接触者追踪、预防或 治疗 措施。它强调了正在进行的研究和保护问题,但没有详细说明任何官方回应。

Bird Flu's Wildlife Toll Raises New Conservation Questions - Nature World News

News Brief
The article titled 'Bird Flu's Wildlife Toll Raises New Conservation Questions' by Nature World News, published on 2026-11-12, discusses the impact of H5N1 bird flu on wildlife, particularly in Papua New Guinea, where it has raised concerns about conservation and extinction risks. While the piece highlights the broader implications of H5N1 on ecosystems, it does not provide specific case or death counts. Instead, it focuses on the broader context of wildlife health and environmental changes, such as the effects of climate change on marine life and the role of fungi in restoring degraded grasslands. The article also touches on other related topics, including the behavior of clownfish during marine heatwaves and the potential of AI in biodiversity research. However, it does not detail any specific response measures or investigation status related to H5N1. The piece serves as a general overview rather than a report on confirmed outbreaks or targeted interventions.
